h:\text{T}^{I}\to\mathbb{T},\qquad\lambda\mapsto\langle h,\lambda\rangle

and for each
\lambda\in\text{N}^{I}
we have the tropical monomial

\lambda:\text{T}^{\vee}_{I}\to\mathbb{T},\qquad h\mapsto\langle h,\lambda\rangle.

4.6.
Hyperplane Arrangements and the Monoid of Semisimple Flats

For
(\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in[2;I]
, the
reflection hyperplane

\mathcal{H}_{(\text{i}_{1}|\text{i}_{2})}\subset\text{T}^{I}
is given by

\mathcal{H}_{(\text{i}_{1}|\text{i}_{2})}:=\big\{\lambda\in\text{T}^{I}\ \big|\ \langle h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}},\lambda\rangle=0\big\}.

This is the tropical variety cut out by the tropical polynomial “
0+h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}}
”. The collection of all reflection hyperplanes in
\text{T}^{I}
is called the
braid arrangement
over
I
. For
(S,T)\in[I;2]
, the
special hyperplane

\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}\subset\text{T}^{\vee}_{I}
is given by

\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}:=\big\{h\in\text{T}^{\vee}_{I}\ \big|\ \langle h,\lambda_{ST}\rangle=0\big\}.

This is the tropical variety cut out by the tropical polynomial “
0+\lambda_{ST}
”. The collection of all special hyperplanes in
\text{T}^{\vee}_{I}
is called the
adjoint braid arrangement
over
I
, which is the adjoint of the braid arrangement in the sense of
[
AM17
]
.

For
F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{k})
a composition of
I
, the
semisimple flat

\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F}\subseteq\text{T}^{\vee}_{I}
is given by

\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F}:=\big\{h\in\text{T}^{\vee}_{I}\ \big|\ \langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle=0\ \text{for}\ 1\leq i\leq k\big\}.

We have the set cospecies
\textsf{T}^{\vee}
given by

\textsf{T}^{\vee}[F]:=\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F}

(\sigma(h))_{\text{i}}:=h_{\sigma^{-1}(\text{i})},\qquad\beta=\text{id}.

Moreover,
\textsf{T}^{\vee}
is a monoid with multiplication the inclusion of semisimple flats,

\mu_{F,G}:\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F}\hookrightarrow\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{G},\qquad h\mapsto h.

Let
\text{T}^{\vee}
denote the Joyalization of
\textsf{T}^{\vee}
. A key fact is that semisimple flats factorize, in the sense that
\textsf{T}^{\vee}
is strongly Joyal-theoretic; we have the isomorphism

\eta:\text{T}^{\vee}\to\textsf{T}^{\vee}

given by

\eta_{F}:\text{T}^{\vee}_{S_{1}}\times\dots\times\text{T}^{\vee}_{S_{k}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F},\qquad\big((h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in S_{1}},\dots,(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in S_{k}}\big)\mapsto(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in I}.

We denote the corresponding multiplication of
\text{T}^{\vee}
by
\text{m}_{F,G}
. Particularly important for us is the multiplication
\text{m}_{F}=\text{m}_{F,(I)}=\mu_{F,(I)}\circ\eta_{F}
, which is given by

\text{m}_{F}:\text{T}^{\vee}_{S_{1}}\times\dots\times\text{T}^{\vee}_{S_{k}}\hookrightarrow\text{T}^{\vee}_{I},\qquad\big((h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in S_{1}},\dots,(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in S_{k}}\big)\mapsto(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in I}.

Remark 4.4
.

Dual to
\textsf{T}^{\vee}
, there is a comonoid
T
, which is the restriction of the comultiplication of tropical permutohedral space to its embedded torus.

4.7.
Cones

Given a subset
X
of
\text{T}^{I}
or
\text{T}_{I}^{\vee}
, we have the conical subspace
\text{Coni}\,X
consisting of all non-negative
\mathbb{R}
-linear combinations of elements of
X
. For
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
a preposet, let

\sigma^{\vee}_{p}:=\text{Coni}\big\{h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in p\big\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sigma^{o}_{p}:=-\sigma^{\vee}_{p}=\text{Coni}\big\{h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{i}_{2},\text{i}_{1})\in p\big\}.

For the distinguished element
\bullet\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
, we put
\sigma^{o}_{\bullet}:=\emptyset
. We have

\sigma^{o}_{q}\subseteq\sigma^{o}_{p}\qquad\iff\qquad p\leq q.

The set of coroots given by
p
is closed under conical combinations:

Proposition 4.2
.

For
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
a preposet, we have

\sigma^{o}_{p}\ \cap\ \big\{h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in[2;I]\big\}\ =\ \big\{h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{i}_{2},\text{i}_{1})\in p\big\}.

Proof.

This follows from e.g.
[
PRW08
, Section 3]
.
∎

Corollary 4.2.1
.

The map
p\mapsto\sigma^{o}_{p}
is a one-to-cone correspondence between preposets and cones which are generated by coroots.

The
\sigma^{o}_{p}
, for
p
a preposet, are generalized permutohedral tangent cones, and the
\sigma^{o}_{F}
, for
F
a total preposet, are permutohedral tangent cones. We have the following important result, which describes the cone
\sigma^{o}_{p}
dually using
[I;2]
instead of
[2;I]
.

Proposition 4.3
.

For
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
a preposet, we have

\sigma^{o}_{p}=\big\{h\in\text{T}_{I}^{\vee}\ \big|\ \langle h,\lambda_{ST}\rangle\leq 0\quad\text{for all}\quad(S,T)\leq p\big\}.

Proof.

See e.g.
[
NO19
, Proposition 3.2]
.
∎

Corollary 4.3.1
.

The polar cone
\sigma_{p}
of
\sigma^{o}_{p}
(
=\,
the dual cone of
\sigma_{p}^{\vee}
) is given by

\sigma_{p}=\text{Coni}\big\{\lambda_{ST}\ \big|\ (S,T)\leq p\big\}.

Proof.

This follows directly from the definition of polar (or dual) cones.
∎

Thus
p\mapsto\sigma_{p}
is a one-to-cone correspondence between preposets and cones (
=
an intersection of halfspaces) of the braid arrangement. The
braid fan
over
I
is the set of conical subspaces given by

\big\{\sigma_{F}:F\in\Sigma[I]\big\}.

The following gives us a geometric interpretation of the multiplication of the bimonoid
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
; it corresponds to taking products of cones.

Proposition 4.4
.

Given preposets
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[S]
and
q\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[T]
, the multiplication
\text{m}_{(S,T)}
restricts to an isomorphism of cones

\text{m}_{(S,T)}:\sigma^{o}_{p}\times\sigma^{o}_{q}\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{o}_{(p\,|\,q)}.

Proof.

The product cone
\sigma^{o}_{p}\times\sigma^{o}_{q}\subseteq\text{T}^{\vee}_{S}\times\text{T}^{\vee}_{T}
is generated by the set

\big\{h_{s_{1}s_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{s}_{1},\text{s}_{2})\in p\big\}\ \sqcup\ \big\{h_{\text{t}_{1}\text{t}_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{t}_{1},\text{t}_{2})\in q\big\}.

The
\text{m}_{(S,T)}
-image of this generating set is the generating set of
\sigma^{o}_{(p\,|\,q)}
,

\text{m}_{(S,T)}\Big(\big\{h_{\text{s}_{1}\text{s}_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{s}_{1},\text{s}_{2})\in p\big\}\ \sqcup\ \big\{h_{\text{t}_{1}\text{t}_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{t}_{1},\text{t}_{2})\in q\big\}\Big)=\big\{h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}}\ \big|\ (\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in(p\,|\,q)\big\}.

Therefore
\sigma^{o}_{p}\times\sigma^{o}_{q}\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{o}_{(p\,|\,q)}
is surjective. It is injective because it is the restriction of an injective map.
∎

More generally, it follows that the multiplication
\text{m}_{F,G}
restricts to an isomorphism

\text{m}_{F,G}:\sigma^{o}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{o}_{\mu_{F,G}(p)}

where
p=(p_{1},\dots,p_{k})\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[F]
and
\sigma^{o}_{p}:=\sigma^{o}_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times\sigma^{o}_{p_{k}}
.

We now attempt something similar for the comultiplication of
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
. Let the
face
of the cone
\sigma^{o}_{p}
in the
\lambda_{ST}
-direction be the region of
\sigma^{o}_{p}
on which the function
\lambda_{ST}
is maximized.

Lemma 4.5
.

If
(S,T)\leq p
, then the face of
\sigma^{o}_{p}
in the
\lambda_{ST}
-direction is given by the intersection

\sigma^{o}_{p}\cap\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}.

If
(S,T)\nleq p
, then
\sigma^{o}_{p}
does not have a face in the
\lambda_{ST}
-direction.

Proof.

Suppose
(S,T)\leq p
. Then
\lambda_{ST}
is non-positive on
\sigma^{o}_{p}
by
4.3
, and
\lambda_{ST}
is
0
on
\mathcal{H}_{(S,T)}
by definition. Finally,
\sigma^{o}_{p}\cap\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}
is nonempty because they both contain the origin.

If
(S,T)\nleq p
, then
(\text{t},\text{s})\in p
for some
\text{s}\in S
and
\text{t}\in T
. Therefore
h_{\text{s}\text{t}}\in\sigma^{o}_{p}
, and so
\lambda_{ST}
obtains arbitrary large values on
\sigma^{o}_{p}
.
∎

The following gives us a geometric interpretation of the comultiplication of
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
; it corresponds to taking faces of cones.

Proposition 4.6
.

Given a preposet
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
and
(S,T)\leq p
, the multiplication
\text{m}_{(S,T)}
restricts to an embedding of cones

\text{m}_{(S,T)}:\sigma^{o}_{p\talloblong_{S}}\times\sigma^{o}_{p\!\fatslash_{\,T}}\hookrightarrow\sigma^{o}_{p}

with image the face of
\sigma^{o}_{p}
in the
\lambda_{ST}
-direction.

Proof.

In light of
4.4
and
4.5
, this amounts to showing

\sigma^{o}_{p}\cap\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}=\sigma^{o}_{(p\talloblong_{S}|p\!\fatslash_{\,T})}.

We have
\sigma^{o}_{p}\cap\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}\supseteq\sigma^{o}_{(p\talloblong_{S}|p\!\fatslash_{\,T})}
since

(\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in\sigma^{o}_{p\talloblong_{S}}\qquad\implies\qquad(\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in p\quad\text{and}\quad\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2}\in S

and

(\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in\sigma^{o}_{p\!\fatslash_{\,T}}\qquad\implies\qquad(\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in p\quad\text{and}\quad\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2}\in T.

To show
\sigma^{o}_{p}\cap\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}\subseteq\sigma^{o}_{(p\talloblong_{S}|p\!\fatslash_{\,T})}
, recall the cone
\sigma^{o}_{p}
may be given as the region satisfying

\langle h,\lambda_{AB}\rangle\leq 0,\qquad\text{for all}\quad(A,B)\leq p.

The intersection
\sigma^{o}_{p}\cap\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}
is then given by adding the extra equality

\langle h,\lambda_{ST}\rangle=0.

On the other hand,
\sigma^{o}_{p\talloblong_{S}}
is the region given by

\langle h,\lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}\rangle\leq 0,\qquad\text{for all}\quad(S_{1},S_{2})\leq p\talloblong_{S}

and
\sigma^{o}_{p\!\fatslash_{\,T}}
is the region given by

\langle h,\lambda_{T_{1}T_{2}}\rangle\leq 0,\qquad\text{for all}\quad(T_{1},T_{2})\leq p\!\!\fatslash_{\,T}.

Let
h\in\sigma^{o}_{p}\cap\mathcal{H}_{(S|T)}
. If
(S_{1},S_{2})\leq p\talloblong_{S}
then
(S_{1},S_{2}\sqcup T)\leq p
, and so

\langle h,\lambda_{S_{1}}\rangle\leq 0.

Similarly, if
(T_{1},T_{2})\leq p\!\fatslash_{\,T}
then
(T_{1}\sqcup S,T_{2})\leq p
, and so

\langle h,\lambda_{T_{1}}\rangle=\langle h,\lambda_{T_{1}}\rangle+\underbrace{\langle h,\lambda_{S}\rangle}_{=0}=\langle h,\lambda_{T_{1}\sqcup S}\rangle\leq 0.\qed

Remark 4.5
.

What about an interpretation of the (co)multiplication of preposets
p
in terms of cones of the braid arrangement? One can view braid arrangement cones
\sigma_{p}
as non-positive loci
\sigma_{p}=(U_{p})_{\leq 0}\cap\text{T}_{I}
of certain open sets of tropical permutohedral space
U_{p}\subseteq\mathbb{T}\Sigma^{I}
(see
Section 6.2
for the definition of
U_{p}
in the complex case). Then the (co)multiplication of braid arrangement cones corresponds to the pullback of opens along the (co)multiplication of
\mathbb{T}\Sigma^{I}
.

5.
The Bimonoid of Permutohedral Space

5.1.
The
\mathsf{{O}}_{0}
-Graded Bialgebra
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}

For
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
, let

\text{M}_{p}:=\sigma^{o}_{p}\cap\text{M}_{I}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{p}]:=\bigoplus_{h\in\text{M}_{p}}\mathbb{C}\cdot f^{h}.

For preposets
p\neq\bullet
,
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{p}]
is a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
with multiplication given by
f^{h_{1}}f^{h_{2}}:=f^{h_{1}+h_{2}}
. If
p=\bullet
, we have the zero vector space
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{\bullet}]=0
. We have the Joyal-theoretic
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
-graded vector cospecies
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
, given by

\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}_{p}[I]:=\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{p}],\qquad\sigma(f^{h}):=f^{h^{\prime}}

and for elements
p,q\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
with
p\leq q
and
p\neq\bullet
, the action of the morphism
q\to p
is the natural inclusion
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{q}]\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{p}]
. If
p=\bullet
, the action of
q\to p
is the unique map into the zero vector space.

We now give
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
the structure of a Joyal-theoretic
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
-graded bialgebra. We shall then use the structure of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
to give permutohedral space the structure of a bimonoid. Once this is done, we may then reinterpret
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
as the bialgebra structure which is induced on the sheaf of regular functions, pulled-back as in
3.1
to the opens of permutohedral space which are indexed by preposets.

For
(S,T)\in[I;2]
,
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[S]
and
q\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[T]
, we have the linear map given by

\Updelta^{\ast}_{p,q}:\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{p}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{q}]\to\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(p|q)}],\qquad f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{h_{2}}\mapsto f^{\text{m}_{(S,T)}(h_{1},h_{2})}.

For
p,q\neq\bullet
, this is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-algebras by
4.4
. For
(S,T)\in[I;2]
and
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
, we have the linear map given by

{\upmu^{\ast}_{S,T}}_{p}:\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{p}]\to\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{p\talloblong_{S}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{p\!\fatslash_{\,T}}],\qquad f^{h}\mapsto\begin{cases}f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{h_{2}}&\quad\text{if}\quad h=\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2})\in\sigma^{o}_{(p\talloblong_{S}|\,p\!\fatslash_{\,T})}\\
0&\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In light of
4.6
, we see that in the case
(S,T)\leq p
this map is sending monomials
f^{h}
for which
h
is in the
\lambda_{ST}
-face of
\sigma_{p}
to their image in the decomposition of that face as a product, and is sending all other monomials to zero. Thus, we may view
{\upmu^{\ast}_{S,T}}_{p}
as a projection onto the
\lambda_{ST}
-face of
\sigma_{p}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

The linear maps
\Updelta^{\ast}_{p,q}
and
{\upmu^{\ast}_{S,T}}_{p}
give
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
the structure of a
Joyal-theoretic

\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
-graded bialgebra.

Proof.

There are seven diagrams to check. The four (co)multiplication naturality diagrams are immediate. For associativity, we require the map

f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{h_{2}}\otimes f^{h_{3}}\mapsto f^{\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2})}\otimes f^{h_{3}}\mapsto f^{\text{m}_{ST,U}(\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2}),h_{3}))}

to equal the map

f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{h_{2}}\otimes f^{h_{3}}\mapsto f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{\text{m}_{T,U}(h_{2},h_{3})}\mapsto f^{\text{m}_{S,TU}(h_{1},\text{m}_{T,U}(h_{2},h_{3}))}.

Indeed, we have

\text{m}_{ST,U}\big(\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2}),h_{3}\big)=\text{m}_{S,TU}\big(h_{1},\text{m}_{T,U}(h_{2},h_{3})\big)

by associativity of the monoid
\text{T}^{\vee}
(defined in
Section 4.6
). For coassociativity, both maps send
f^{h}
to zero unless

h=\text{m}_{ST,U}\big(\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2}),h_{3}\big)=\text{m}_{S,TU}\big(h_{1},\text{m}_{T,U}(h_{2},h_{3})\big)

for some
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
. Then the map

f^{h}=f^{\text{m}_{ST,U}(\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2}),h_{3})}\mapsto f^{\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2})}\otimes f^{h_{3}}\mapsto f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{h_{2}}\otimes f^{h_{3}}

is equal to the map

f^{h}=f^{\text{m}_{S,TU}(h_{1},\text{m}_{T,U}(h_{2},h_{3}))}\mapsto f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{\text{m}_{T,U}(h_{2},h_{3})}\mapsto f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{h_{2}}\otimes f^{h_{3}}.

For the bimonoid axiom, both maps send
f^{h}\otimes f^{k}
to zero unless

h=\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2})\qquad\text{and}\qquad k=\text{m}_{U,V}(k_{1},k_{2})

for some
h_{1},h_{2}
and
k_{1},k_{2}
. Then the map

f^{\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2})}\otimes f^{\text{m}_{U,V}(k_{1},k_{2})}\mapsto f^{\text{m}_{ST,UV}(\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2}),\text{m}_{U,V}(k_{1},k_{2}))}=f^{\text{m}_{SU,TV}(\text{m}_{S,U}(h_{1},k_{1}),\text{m}_{T,V}(h_{2},k_{2}))}

\mapsto f^{\text{m}_{S,U}(h_{1},k_{1})}\otimes f^{\text{m}_{T,V}(h_{2},k_{2})}

is equal to the map

f^{\text{m}_{S,T}(h_{1},h_{2})}\otimes f^{\text{m}_{U,V}(k_{1},k_{2})}\mapsto f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{h_{2}}\otimes f^{k_{1}}\otimes f^{k_{2}}\mapsto f^{h_{1}}\otimes f^{k_{1}}\otimes f^{h_{2}}\otimes f^{k_{2}}

\mapsto f^{\text{m}_{S,U}(h_{1},k_{1})}\otimes f^{\text{m}_{T,V}(h_{2},k_{2})}.\qed

5.2.
The
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-Graded Bimonoid
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}

We now describe the bimonoid structure of permutohedral space on an atlas of affine charts, before gluing them together in the next section. We define a
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-graded
bimonoid
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
which consists of homomorphisms on the
\mathbb{C}
-algebras of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
, with structure maps the pullback of homomorphisms along the corresponding structure maps of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
(although the comultiplication
{\Updelta_{S,T}}_{H}
is not quite just this, see below).

For
H
a composition, we denote the affine variety which is the set of
\mathbb{C}
-points of the affine scheme
\text{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}])
by
U_{H}
, thus

U_{H}:=\big\{\text{$\mathbb{C}$-algebra homomorphisms }x:\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]\to\mathbb{C}\big\}.

We have the Joyal-theoretic
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-graded set species
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
, given by

\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}_{H}[I]:=U_{H},\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}_{H}[\sigma](x):=x\circ\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}_{H}[\sigma],\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}_{K\to H}[I](x):=x\circ\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}_{H\to K}[I]=x|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]}.

The functoriality of
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
then follows directly from the functoriality of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
and the fact that pullback of homomorphisms along functions
f
is functorial in
f
.

We now give
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
the structure of a Joyal-theoretic
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-graded bimonoid. For
(S,T)\in[I;2]
,
H\in\Sigma[S]
and
K\in\Sigma[T]
, we have the function
14
14

14

here we use the fact that the (total preposet of the) concatenation
H;K
satisfies
(H;K)\talloblong_{S}=H
and
(H;K)\!\fatslash_{\,T}=K
, i.e. the restriction of the comultiplication of
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
to
\mathsf{\Sigma}^{\ast}
is deconcatenation

\upmu_{H,K}:U_{H}\times U_{K}\to U_{H;K},\qquad\upmu_{H,K}(x_{1},x_{2}):=(x_{1}\otimes x_{2})\circ{\upmu^{\ast}_{S,T}}_{H;K}.

For
(S,T)\in[I;2]
and
H\in\Sigma[I]
, we have the function

{\Updelta_{S,T}}_{H}:U_{H}\to U_{H|_{S}}\times U_{H|_{T}}

{\Updelta_{S,T}}_{H}(x):=x\circ\big((H|_{S}\,|\,H|_{T})\to H\big)\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{H|_{S},H|_{T}}=(x|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H|_{S}}]},x|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H|_{T}}]})

where
((H|_{S}\,|\,H|_{T})\to H)
abbreviates
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}_{(H|_{S}\,|\,H|_{T})\to H}[I]
.

Proposition 5.2
.

The maps
\upmu_{H,K}
and
{\Updelta_{S,T}}_{H}
give
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
the structure of a
Joyal-theoretic

\mathsf{\Sigma}
-graded bimonoid.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the structure maps of
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
are just pullbacks along the structure maps of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
, which we know satisfies the axioms of a bialgebra. This is immediate, except when the comultiplication is involved since this is not quite just the pullback, and we must make use of comultiplication
\mathtt{f}
-naturality of
\mathbb{C}\textsf{{O}}
as well.

We make the bimonoid axiom explicit, with coassociativity following similarly. We have

{\upmu^{\ast}_{ST,UV}}_{H\ast K}\circ\big((H\ast K|_{SU}\,|\,H\ast K|_{TV})\to H\ast K\big)\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{H\ast K|_{SU},H\ast K|_{TV}}

=\underbrace{\big((H|_{S}\,|\,H|_{T})\to H\otimes(K|_{U}\,|\,K|_{V})\to K\big)\circ{\upmu^{\ast}_{ST,UV}}_{(H\ast K)|_{SU}\,|\,(H\ast K)|_{TV})}}_{\text{comultiplication $\mathtt{f}$-naturality of $\mathbb{C}\textsf{{O}}$}}\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{(H\ast K)|_{SU},(H\ast K)|_{TV}}

=\big((H|_{S}\,|\,H|_{T})\to H\otimes(K|_{U}\,|\,K|_{V})\to K\big)\circ\underbrace{\big(\Updelta^{\ast}_{H|_{S},H|_{T}}\otimes\Updelta^{\ast}_{K|_{U},K|_{V}}\big)\circ B\circ\big({\upmu^{\ast}_{S,U}}_{H|_{S},K_{U}}\otimes{\upmu^{\ast}_{T,V}}_{H|_{T},K|_{V}}\big)}_{\text{bimonoid axiom of $\mathbb{C}\textsf{{O}}$}}

=\big((H|_{S}\,|\,H|_{T})\to H\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{H|_{S},H|_{T}}\otimes(K|_{U}\,|\,K|_{V})\to K\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{K|_{U},K|_{V}}\big)\circ B\circ\big({\upmu^{\ast}_{S,U}}_{H|_{S},K_{U}}\otimes{\upmu^{\ast}_{T,V}}_{H_{T},K|_{V}}\big).

To write the second line we used that e.g.
(H\ast K)|_{S}=H|_{S}
because
K
is a composition of
UV
, and similarly e.g.
(H\ast K)|_{ST}=H
.
∎

5.3.
The Bimonoid of Complex Permutohedral Space

We call
complex permutohedral space
over
I
the
\mathbb{C}
-scheme which is the colimit of the diagram

\Sigma[I]\to\textsf{Aff}_{\mathbb{C}},\qquad H\mapsto\text{Spec}\big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]\big),\quad(K\to H)\mapsto(\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}_{H\to K}[I])^{\ast}.

Let
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
denote the algebraic variety which is the set of
\mathbb{C}
-points of complex permutohedral space, thus

\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}:=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{H\in\Sigma[I]}U_{H}=\bigsqcup_{H\in\Sigma[I]}U_{H}\ \Big/\sim

where the equivalence is given by

x\in U_{H}\sim y\in U_{K}\iff\exists z\in U_{H\cup K}\text{ with }z|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]}=x\text{ and }z|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]}=y.

The algebraic variety
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
is the toric variety which is the compactification of the torus
\mathbb{C}\text{T}^{I}
with respect to the braid fan
\{\sigma_{H}\ |\ H\in\Sigma[I]\}
. The affine charts
U_{H}\subset\mathbb{C}\Sigma
are open sets, however as open sets we shall come to index them by total preposets as opposed to compositions.

We have the Joyal-theoretic set species
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
, given by

\mathbb{C}\Sigma[I]:=\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I},\qquad\mathbb{C}\Sigma[\sigma](x):=x\circ\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}_{H}[\sigma]

where
x\in U_{H}
. The action of bijections is well-defined by
(\sigma,\mathtt{f})
-functoriality of
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
; if
x\in U_{H}
and
y\in U_{K}
such that there exists
z\in U_{H\cup K}
with
z|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]}=x
and
z|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]}=y
, then
z^{\prime}\in U_{H^{\prime}\cup K^{\prime}}
with
z^{\prime}|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}]}=x^{\prime}
and
z^{\prime}|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K^{\prime}}]}=y^{\prime}
.

We let

\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}:=\mathbb{C}\Sigma[F]=\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S_{1}}\times\dots\times\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S_{k}}

and denote the projections by

\pi_{S_{j}}:\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}\to\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S_{j}}.

We now give
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
the structure of a connected bimonoid by ‘quotienting out’ the grading of
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
. Then the multiplication is given by

\upmu_{S,T}:\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S}\times\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{T}\to\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I},\qquad(x_{1},x_{2})\mapsto\upmu_{H,K}(x_{1},x_{2})

where
H\in\Sigma[S]
,
K\in\Sigma[T]
such that
(x_{1},x_{2})\in U_{H}\times U_{K}
, and the comultiplication is given by

\Updelta_{S,T}:\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}\to\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S}\times\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{T},\qquad x\mapsto{\Updelta_{S,T}}_{H}(x)

where
H\in\Sigma[I]
such that
x\in U_{H}
.

Theorem 5.3
.

Equipped with the maps
\upmu_{S,T}
and
\Delta_{S,T}
as defined above, the set species of permutohedral space
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
is a Joyal-theoretic bimonoid.

Proof.

The maps
\upmu_{S,T}
and
\Delta_{S,T}
are well-defined for the quotient
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{H\in\Sigma[I]}U_{H}
by (co)multiplication
\mathtt{f}
-naturality of
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
, analogous to what we saw above with bijections
\sigma
. The diagrams for a bimonoid commute for
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
because they commute for
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
.

Alternatively, one can use the Losev-Manin realization of permutohedral space as a moduli space to easily prove this theorem, mentioned in the introduction.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

Here, we are taking the colimit over the
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-grading of the bimonoid
\widetilde{\mathbb{C}\Sigma}
to give an ungraded bimonoid
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
, analogous to taking the vector space
\bigoplus_{i}V_{i}\in\textsf{Vec}
given a graded vector space
(V_{i})\in\textsf{gVec}
. The functoriality and naturality axioms of a graded bimonoid means this is always possible; one can always ‘quotient out’ the grading of a graded bimonoid to get an ordinary bimonoid. For example, the colimit of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
is rather uninteresting; it has the zero vector space in each component.

5.4.
Torus Orbits

For each composition
H=(S_{1},\dots,S_{k})
of
I
, we have the
torus orbit

V_{H}\subseteq U_{H}\subset\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
given by

V_{H}:=\big\{x\in U_{H}\ \big|\ x(f^{h})=0\quad\text{for all}\quad h\notin\sigma^{o}_{(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k})}\big\}

where
(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k})
denotes the preposet
p
given by
(\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in p
if
\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2}\in S_{j}
for some
1\leq j\leq k
. We have

\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}=\bigsqcup_{H\in\Sigma[I]}V_{H}\qquad\text{and}\qquad U_{H}=\bigsqcup_{K\leq H}V_{K}.

Because torus orbits are disjoint, it is useful to describe the (co)multiplication of permutohedral space in terms of torus orbits.

Lemma 5.4
.

Given
(S,T)\in[I;2]
, the multiplication
\upmu_{S,T}:\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S}\times\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{T}\to\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
restricts to isomorphisms

\upmu_{S,T}:V_{H}\times V_{K}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{H;K}.

Proof.

Recall the comultiplication of
\mathbb{C}\textsf{{O}}

{\upmu^{\ast}_{S,T}}_{H;K}:\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H;K}]\to\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]

which maps the
\lambda_{S,T}
-face
\sigma^{o}_{(H|K)}
to its decomposition as a product, and sends everything else to zero. We have that
\sigma^{o}_{(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k}|T_{1}|\dots|T_{l})}
is contained within the
\lambda_{S,T}
-face, and so the restriction of
{\upmu^{\ast}_{S,T}}_{H;K}
to the subalgebra
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k}|T_{1}|\dots|T_{l})}]
is an isomorphism

\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k}|T_{1}|\dots|T_{l})}]\to\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k})}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(T_{1}|\dots|T_{l})}]

which, via the identification

V_{H}\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Hom}\!\big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k})}],\mathbb{C}\big),\qquad x\mapsto x|_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k})}]}

and similar identifications for
V_{K}
and
V_{H;K}
, is dual to
V_{H}\times V_{K}\to V_{H;K}
.

Alternatively, one can use the Losev-Manin realization to easily prove this result.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Given
(S,T)\in[I;2]
, the comultiplication
\Updelta_{S,T}:\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}\to\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S}\times\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{T}
restricts to surjections

\Updelta_{S,T}:V_{H}\to V_{H|_{S}}\times V_{H|_{T}}.

Proof.

First we show that
V_{H}\to V_{H|_{S}}\times V_{H|_{T}}
is well-defined. Recall

\Updelta_{S,T}(x)=(x|_{\mathbb{C}[M_{H|_{S}}]},x|_{\mathbb{C}[M_{H|_{T}}]}).

Given
(\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in H|_{S}
such that
(\text{i}_{2},\text{i}_{1})\notin H|_{S}
, we have
(\text{i}_{1},\text{i}_{2})\in H
with
(\text{i}_{2},\text{i}_{1})\notin H
. Therefore, for
x\in V_{H}
, we have

x|_{\mathbb{C}[M_{H|_{S}}]}(f^{h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}}})=\underbrace{x(f^{h_{\text{i}_{1}\text{i}_{2}}})=0}_{\text{because $x\in V_{H}$}}.

Thus
x|_{\mathbb{C}[M_{H|_{S}}]}\in V_{H|_{S}}
. We have
x|_{\mathbb{C}[M_{H|_{T}}]}\in V_{H|_{T}}
by the same argument. For surjectivity, we have

\sigma^{\circ}_{\big(S_{1}\cap S|\dots|S_{k}\cap S\big|S_{1}\cap T|\dots|S_{k}\cap T\big)}\subset\sigma^{\circ}_{(S_{1}|\dots|S_{k})},

and so every homomorphism in
V_{H|_{S}}\times V_{H|_{T}}
can be extended to a homomorphism in
V_{H}
.

Alternatively, one can use the Losev-Manin realization to easily prove this result.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

Thus,
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
is isomorphic to the species of
closed torus orbits

F\mapsto\overline{V}_{F}:=\bigsqcup_{F\leq H}V_{H}

with multiplication given simply by inclusion of closed torus orbits. This way of doing things is nice because the key fact about permutohedral space being a space with factorization of its boundaries is formalized as the statement that the species
F\mapsto\overline{V}_{F}
is strongly Joyal-theoretic.

6.
Opens and Preposets

We define the bimonoid in cospecies of Zariski open sets
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
, and we realize the structure sheaf as an
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
-graded
bialgebra. This recovers
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
as the pullback of the grading along indexing certain opens by preposets.

6.1.
The Bimonoid of Opens

For
F
a composition, let
\textsf{Op}_{F}
be the pointed category with objects Zariski open sets
U\subseteq\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
, morphisms inclusion of open sets
U\hookrightarrow V
, and distinguished object the empty set
\emptyset\subseteq\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
. We have the
\textsf{Cat}_{\bullet}
-valued cospecies of opens
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
, given by

\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}[F]:=\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}_{F},

\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}[\sigma,F](U):=\big(\mathbb{C}\Sigma[\sigma,F]\big)^{-1}(U),\qquad\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}[F,\beta](U):=\big(\mathbb{C}\Sigma[F,\beta]\big)^{-1}(U).

This is a strict cospecies. We abbreviate
U^{\prime}=\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}[\sigma,F](U)
and
\widetilde{U}=\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}[F,\beta](U)
as usual.

Let
\text{Op}^{\text{op}}
denote the Joyalization of
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
. We give
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
the structure of a weakly
Joyal-theoretic
cospecies by taking cartesian products of open sets,

\text{prod}:\text{Op}^{\text{op}}\to\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}},\qquad(U_{1},\dots,U_{k})\,\mapsto\,U_{1}\times\dots\times U_{k}:=\pi_{S_{1}}^{-1}(U_{1})\cap\dots\cap\pi_{S_{k}}^{-1}(U_{k}).

Note this map commutes with the action of
\sigma
and
\beta
on the nose, so it is a strict morphism.

We now give
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
the structure of a bimonoid. We have the functors the pullback of opens along the (co)multiplication of permutohedral space,

\Updelta^{-1}_{F,G}:\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}_{F}\to\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}_{G},\qquad U\mapsto\Updelta^{-1}_{F,G}(U)

and

\upmu^{-1}_{F,G}:\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}_{G}\to\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}_{F},\qquad U\mapsto\upmu^{-1}_{F,G}(U).

Proposition 6.1
.

The functors
\Updelta^{-1}_{F,G}
and
\upmu^{-1}_{F,G}
give opens of permutohedral space
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
the structure of a bimonoid in
\textsf{Cat}_{\bullet}
-valued
cospecies. This is a strict bimonoid.

Proof.

This follows directly from the fact that
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
is a bimonoid, together with the fact that pullback of sets
X
along functions
f
is functorial in
f
, i.e.

(g\circ f)^{-1}(X)=f^{-1}\big(g^{-1}(X)\big).\qed

Remark 6.1
.

Note the self-dual nature of the bimonoid axiom; it is the diagram
\text{B}_{G,F,A}
of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
which implies the diagram
\text{B}_{F,G,A}
of
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
.

6.2.
The Structure Sheaf Bialgebra and the Restriction to Preposets

We can model the structure sheaf of permutohedral space as the
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
-graded
vector cospecies

\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}
given by taking regular functions, pulling them back along the corresponding maps of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
, and restricting them along inclusions
V\hookleftarrow U
,

\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}_{U}[F]:=\big\{\text{regular functions }f:U\to\mathbb{C}\big\}

\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}_{U}[\sigma,F](f):=f\circ\mathbb{C}\Sigma[\sigma,F],\quad\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}_{U}[F,\beta](f):=f\circ\mathbb{C}\Sigma[F,\beta],\quad\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}_{V\hookleftarrow U}[F,F](f):=f|_{U}.

The functoriality of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}
follows directly from the fact that functions commute with inclusion of subsets and the functoriality of
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
, together with the fact that pullback of functions along functions
f
is functorial in
f
.

We have the linear maps the pullback of regular functions along the (co)multiplication of permutohedral space,

\Updelta_{F,G}^{\ast}:\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}_{U}[F]\to\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}_{\Updelta_{F,G}^{-1}(U)}[G],\qquad f\mapsto\Updelta_{F,G}^{\ast}f:=f\circ\Updelta_{F,G}

and

\upmu_{F,G}^{\ast}:\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}_{U}[G]\to\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}_{\upmu_{F,G}^{-1}(U)}[F],\qquad f\mapsto\upmu_{F,G}^{\ast}f:=f\circ\upmu_{F,G}.

Proposition 6.2
.

The maps
\Updelta_{F,G}^{\ast}
and
\upmu_{F,G}^{\ast}
give
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Op}}^{\text{op}}
the structure of a
\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
-graded
bialgebra in cospecies.

Proof.

We have that (co)multiplication
\mathtt{f}
-naturality follows directly from the fact that functions commute with inclusion of subsets, together with the fact that pullback of functions along functions
f
is functorial in
f
. Everything else follows directly from the fact that
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
is a bimonoid, again together with the fact that pullback of functions along functions is functorial.
∎

We can index certain opens of permutohedral space
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
with preposets; we have the
Joyal-theoretic
morphism of cospecies given by

U_{(-)}:\textsf{O}_{\bullet}\to\text{Op}^{\text{op}},\qquad p\mapsto U_{p}:=\bigcup_{F\leq p}U_{F}=\bigsqcup_{F\leq p}V_{F}.

In particular, we have
U_{\bullet}=\emptyset
. This is a strict morphism.

Remark 6.2
.

Notice
U_{p}
is naturally the toric variety of the subfan
\{\sigma_{F}\ |\ F\leq p\}
of the braid fan.

Theorem 6.3
.

The composition of morphisms

\varphi:\textsf{O}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{U_{(-)}}\text{Op}^{\text{op}}\xrightarrow{\text{prod}}\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}},\qquad(p_{1},\dots,p_{k})\mapsto U_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k}}

is a strict homomorphism of bimonoids.

Proof.

For compositions
G\leq F
, let

F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{k})\qquad\text{and}\qquad G=(T_{1},\dots,T_{l}).

Let
1\leq\mathtt{i}_{j}\leq k
be the integer such that
S_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}
is the
\mathtt{i}
th lump of the restriction of
F
to
T_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq l
. Thus,

\mathtt{i}_{j}:=k(1)+\,\cdots\,+k(j-1)+\mathtt{i}

where
k(j):=l(F|_{T_{j}})
.

Preservation of multiplication says that given an element
p=(p_{1},\dots,p_{k})\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[F]
, we should have

(11)

\Updelta^{-1}_{F,G}(U_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k}})=U_{(p_{1_{1}}|\dots|p_{k(1)_{1}})}\times\dots\times U_{(p_{1_{l}}|\dots|p_{k(l)_{l}})}.

However, the pullback of product opens along the comultiplication
\Updelta_{F,G}
factorizes as follows,

\Updelta^{-1}_{F,G}(U_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k}})=(\Delta_{F|_{T_{1}}}\times\dots\times\Updelta_{F|_{T_{l}}})^{-1}(U_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k}})

=\Updelta^{-1}_{F|_{T_{1}}}(U_{p_{1_{1}}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k(1)_{1}}})\times\cdots\cdots\times\Updelta^{-1}_{F|_{T_{l}}}(U_{p_{1_{l}}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k(l)_{l}}}).

Therefore it is enough to check
(
11
)
in the case
G=(I)
, which is

\Updelta^{-1}_{F}(U_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k}})=U_{(p_{1}|\dots|p_{k})}.

We have

U_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k}}=\bigsqcup_{H_{i}\leq p_{i}}V_{H_{1}}\times\dots\times V_{H_{k}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad U_{(p_{1}|\dots|p_{k})}=\bigsqcup_{H\leq(p_{1}|\dots|p_{k})}V_{H}.

Let
x\in V_{H}\subset\mathbb{C}\Sigma[I]
. If
H\leq(p_{1}|\dots|p_{k})
, then
H_{i}|_{S_{i}}\leq p_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
, and so

\Updelta_{F}(x)\in V_{H|_{S_{1}}}\times\dots\times V_{H|_{S_{k}}}\subset U_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k}}.

Conversely, if
\Updelta_{F}(x)\in U_{p_{1}}\times\dots U_{p_{k}}
, then

(V_{H|_{S_{1}}}\times\dots\times V_{H|_{S_{k}}})\ \cap\ (U_{p_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{p_{k}})\neq\emptyset.

But torus orbits are disjoint. Therefore
H|_{S_{i}}\leq p_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
, and so
H\leq(p_{1}|\dots|p_{k})
.

The pullback of product opens along the multiplication
\upmu_{F,G}
factorizes similarly, and so it is enough to check the preservation of comultiplication in the case
G=(I)
. This says given an element
p\in\textsf{O}_{\bullet}[I]
, we have

\upmu^{-1}_{F}(U_{p})=\begin{cases}U_{p\talloblong_{S_{1}}}\times\dots\times U_{p\talloblong_{S_{k}}}&\quad\text{if}\ F\leq p\\
\emptyset&\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

The image of the multiplication
\upmu_{F}
is all those torus orbits
V_{H}\subset\mathbb{C}\Sigma[I]
such that
H
is obtained from a concatenation over
F
, that is

\operatorname{image}(\upmu_{F})=\bigsqcup_{H_{i}\in\Sigma[S_{i}]}V_{H_{1};\cdots;H_{k}}.

We have
V_{H_{1};\cdots;H_{k}}\subseteq U_{p}
if and only if
H_{1};\cdots;H_{k}\leq p
, therefore by
5.4
we have

\upmu^{-1}_{F}(U_{p})=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}H_{i}\in\Sigma[S_{i}]\\
H_{1};\cdots;H_{k}\leq p\end{subarray}}V_{H_{1}}\times\dots\times V_{H_{k}}.

If
F\leq p
, then

H_{1};\cdots;H_{k}\leq p\quad\iff\quad H_{i}\leq p\!\talloblong_{S_{i}}\quad\text{for all}\quad 1\leq i\leq k.

Therefore

\upmu^{-1}_{F}(U_{p})=\bigsqcup_{H_{i}\leq p\talloblong_{S_{i}}}V_{H_{1}}\times\dots\times V_{H_{k}}=\bigsqcup_{H_{1}\leq p\talloblong_{S_{1}}}V_{H_{1}}\times\dots\times\bigsqcup_{H_{k}\leq p\talloblong_{S_{k}}}V_{H_{k}}\ =\ U_{p\talloblong_{S_{1}}}\times\dots\times U_{p\talloblong_{S_{k}}}

as required. Now suppose
F\nleq p
. However, we always have

H\leq H_{1};\cdots;H_{k}\qquad\text{and}\qquad H_{1};\cdots;H_{k}\leq p.

Therefore in this case there can be no such concatenations
H_{1};\cdots;H_{k}
, and so

\upmu^{-1}_{F}(U_{p})=\emptyset

as required.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

Notice the pullback
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
-graded bialgebra along the homomorphism
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}\to\textsf{Op}^{\text{op}}
, as constructed in
3.1
, is (naturally isomorphic to)
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
.

7.
Sheaves of Modules

7.1.
The Cospecies of Sheaves

We recall some basic aspects of sheaves on permutohedral space, in particular the étale space perspective on sheaves.

For
F
a composition, let
\textsf{Top}/\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
denote the slice category of topological bundles over
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
, where
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
is equipped with the Zariski topology. Explicitly, objects of
\textsf{Top}/\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
are topological spaces
E
equipped with continuous maps

\pi:E\to\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}

and morphisms are continuous maps
\varphi:E_{1}\to E_{2}
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

commutes. An
étale space
over
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
is a topological bundle
E\to\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
which is also a local homeomorphism. For each point
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
, we call

E(x):=\pi^{-1}(x)

the
stalk
of
E
at
x
. For each open
U\subseteq\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
, we have the set
\Gamma_{U}(E)
of
sections
of
E
over
U
, given by

\Gamma_{U}(E):=\big\{\text{continuous maps}\ s:U\to E\ \big|\ \pi\circ s=\text{id}_{U}\big\}.

We have the presheaf
\mathcal{F}_{E}
on
\textsf{Op}_{F}
associated to
E
, given by

\mathcal{F}_{E}:\textsf{Op}_{F}^{\text{\text{op}}}\to\textsf{Set},\qquad U\mapsto\Gamma_{U}(E),\quad(V\hookrightarrow U)\mapsto(s\mapsto s|_{V}).

Moreover,
\mathcal{F}_{E}
satisfies locality and gluing, and so is in fact a sheaf.

Going the other way, consider a generic presheaf on
\textsf{Op}_{F}
,

\mathcal{F}:\textsf{Op}_{F}^{\text{\text{op}}}\to\textsf{Set}.

We still call elements
s\in\mathcal{F}(U)

sections
, and denote
s|_{V}:=\mathcal{F}(V\hookrightarrow U)(s)\in\mathcal{F}(V)
. For each (possibly non-open) subset
X\subseteq\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
, let

\mathcal{F}(X):=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{X\subseteq U}\mathcal{F}(U)=\bigsqcup_{X\subseteq U}\mathcal{F}(U)\ \ \Big/\sim

where the equivalence is given by

s_{1}\in\mathcal{F}(U_{1})\sim s_{2}\in\mathcal{F}(U_{2})\iff\text{there exists $W\in\textsf{Op}_{F}$ such that $X\subseteq W\subseteq U_{1}\cap U_{2}$ and $s_{1}|_{W}=s_{2}|_{W}$}.

For each point
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
, the set

\mathcal{F}(x):=\mathcal{F}\big(\{x\}\big)

is called the
stalk
of
\mathcal{F}
at
x
. The image of a section
s\in\mathcal{F}(U)
in the stalk at a point
x\in U
is called the
germ

s_{x}\in\mathcal{F}(x)
of
s
at
x
. We have the topological bundle
E_{\mathcal{F}}\to\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
associated to
\mathcal{F}
, which has stalks the stalks of
\mathcal{F}

E_{\mathcal{F}}(x):=\mathcal{F}(x)

and topology with basis

\Big\{(U,s):=\big\{s_{x}\ \big|\ x\in U\big\}\ \Big|\ U\in\textsf{Op}_{F},\ s\in\mathcal{F}(U)\Big\}.

These constructions extend to an idempotent adjunction between topological bundles and presheaves, which restricts to an equivalence between étale spaces and sheaves. Thus, when working with sheaves, we can treat them as certain presheaves, or as certain topological bundles. From now on, let us also refer to étale spaces as sheaves.

Let
\textsf{Sh}_{F}
denote the full subcategory of
\textsf{Top}/\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
of sheaves over
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
. We have the
Cat
-valued
cospecies of sheaves
Sh
, given by pulling back bundles with respect to the corresponding maps of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
(i.e. the so-called inverse image functor),

\textsf{Sh}[F]:=\textsf{Sh}_{F}

\textsf{Sh}[F,\sigma](E):=\sigma^{-1}E=E\times_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F^{\prime}}=\lim(E\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}\xleftarrow{\sigma}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F^{\prime}})

\textsf{Sh}[\beta,F](E):=\beta^{-1}E=E\times_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}=\lim(E\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}\xleftarrow{\beta}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}).

In fact, this can easily be formalized as a strict cospecies, since pullback sheaves have stalks equal to stalks of the original sheaf. We may give
Sh
the structure of a bimonoid by pulling back sheaves with respect to the (co)multiplication of of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
,

\Updelta^{-1}_{F,G}:\textsf{Sh}_{F}\to\textsf{Sh}_{G},\qquad\Updelta^{-1}_{F,G}E=E\times_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}=\lim\big(E\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}\xleftarrow{\Updelta_{F,G}}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}\big)

and

\upmu^{-1}_{F,G}:\textsf{Sh}_{G}\to\textsf{Sh}_{F},\qquad\upmu^{-1}_{F,G}E=E\times_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}=\lim\big(E\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}\xleftarrow{\upmu_{F,G}}\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}\big).

We will not use this bimonoid structure. We are interested in the bimonoid structure on sheaves of modules, and in particular invertible sheaves.

7.2.
The Cospecies of Sheaves of Modules

The category of sheaves
\textsf{Sh}_{F}
is cartesian monoidal by taking fibered products,

E_{1}\times E_{2}:=E_{1}\times_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}E_{2}.

If we model sheaves as certain presheaves, this monoidal product corresponds to the usual cartesian product of presheaves, given by

\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}(U)=\mathcal{F}(U)\times\mathcal{G}(U).

Let
\mathcal{R}\in\textsf{Sh}_{F}
be an internal ring. In particular, this means stalks
\mathcal{R}(x)
and sections
\Gamma_{U}(\mathcal{R})
have the structure of usual rings. Let
M
and
N
be
\mathcal{R}
-modules. In particular, this means stalks
M(x)
and sections
\Gamma_{U}(M)
have the structure of
\mathcal{R}(x)
-modules and
\Gamma_{U}(\mathcal{R})
-modules respectively. The
tensor product
of
\mathcal{R}
-modules
M\otimes N=M\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}N
is the sheaf (=étale space) of the presheaf

U\mapsto\Gamma_{U}(M)\otimes_{\Gamma_{U}(\mathcal{R})}\Gamma_{U}(N).

Explicitly, the stalks of
M\otimes N
are given by pointwise tensoring

M\otimes N(x):=M(x)\otimes N(x)=M(x)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}(x)}N(x)

and the topology has basis

\bigg\{\Big\{\sum_{i}s^{i}_{x}\otimes t^{i}_{x}\ \Big|\ x\in U\Big\}\ \bigg|\ U\in\textsf{Op}_{F},\ \sum_{i}s^{i}\otimes t^{i}\in\Gamma_{U}(M)\otimes_{\Gamma_{U}(\mathcal{R})}\Gamma_{U}(N)\bigg\}.

Here
\sum_{i}s^{i}\otimes t^{i}
is denoting a generic element of
\Gamma_{U}(M)\otimes_{\Gamma_{U}(\mathcal{R})}\Gamma_{U}(N)
as a sum of simple tensors.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{F}
denote the function field of rational functions on
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
. The
structure sheaf

\mathcal{O}_{F}\in\textsf{Sh}_{F}
is the internal ring with stalks given by

\mathcal{O}_{F}(x):=\big\{g/f\in\mathcal{K}_{F}\ \big|\ f(x)\neq 0\big\}

and topology with basis

\Big\{(U,g):=\big\{\text{germ}_{x}(g)\ \big|\ x\in U\big\}\ \Big|\ U\in\textsf{Op}_{F},\ g:U\to\mathbb{C}\text{ a regular function}\Big\}.

We have

\Gamma_{U}(\mathcal{O}_{F})=\big\{\text{regular functions }g:U\to\mathbb{C}\big\}.

Let
\textsf{Mod}_{F}
denote the category of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-modules. We have the
Cat
-valued
cospecies of modules
Mod
, given by pulling back modules along the corresponding maps of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
,

\textsf{Mod}[F]:=\textsf{Mod}_{F}

\textsf{Mod}[\sigma,F](M):=\mathbb{C}\Sigma[\sigma,F]^{\ast}M=\sigma^{-1}M\otimes_{\sigma^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}

\textsf{Mod}[F,\beta](M):=\mathbb{C}\Sigma[F,\beta]^{\ast}M=\beta^{-1}M\otimes_{\beta^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}.

We abbreviate
M^{\prime}=\textsf{Mod}[\sigma,F](M)
and
\widetilde{M}=\textsf{Mod}[\beta,F](M)
as usual. We still need to define three
2
-cells

(\sigma,\beta)
,
(\sigma,\tau)
and
(\beta,\gamma)
. They are just the canonical isomorphisms which express how pullback of sheaves of modules along maps
f
is functorial in
f
. We now make them explicit.

Given a bijection
\sigma:J\to I
and a permutation
\beta\in\text{Sym}_{k}
, for each
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-module
M
,
F\in\Sigma[J]
,
l(F)=k
, we need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}^{\prime}}
-modules

(\sigma,\beta)_{M}:\widetilde{(M^{\prime})}\to(\widetilde{M})^{\prime}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{\widetilde{F}^{\prime}}
,
(\sigma,\beta)_{M}
is given by

{(\sigma,\beta)_{M}}|_{x}:M(\tilde{x}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}(\tilde{x})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}^{\prime}}(x)\to M(\tilde{x}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}(x^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}^{\prime}}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

Here,
\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}
denotes the pullback of the germ
\text{f}_{1}
along
\beta=\mathbb{C}\Sigma[\widetilde{F}^{\prime},\beta]
. The inverse of
(\sigma,\beta)
, which we need for the bimonoid axiom, is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

Similarly,
\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}
denotes the pullback of the germ
\text{f}_{1}
along
\sigma=\mathbb{C}\Sigma[\sigma,\widetilde{F}^{\prime}]
. Given composable bijections
K\xrightarrow{\tau}J\xrightarrow{\sigma}I
, for each
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-module
M
,
F\in\Sigma[K]
, we need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime\prime}}
-modules

(\sigma,\tau)_{M}:(M^{\prime})^{\prime}\to M^{\prime\prime}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma[F^{\prime\prime}]
,
(\sigma,\tau)_{M}
is given by

{(\sigma,\tau)_{M}}|_{x}:M(x^{\prime\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime\prime}}(x)\to M(x^{\prime\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime\prime}}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

Given permutations
\beta,\gamma\in\text{Sym}_{k}
, for each
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-module
M
,
l(F)=k
, we need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{F}}}
-modules

(\beta,\gamma)_{M}:\widetilde{(\widetilde{M})}\to\widetilde{\widetilde{M}}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{\widetilde{\widetilde{F}}}
,
(\beta,\gamma)_{M}
is given by

{(\beta,\gamma)_{M}}|_{x}:M(\tilde{\tilde{x}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}(\tilde{x})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{F}}}(x)\to M(\tilde{\tilde{x}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\widetilde{F}}}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

We now need to check the four coherence laws of cospecies.

Proposition 7.1
.

Equipped with the
2
-cells
(\sigma,\beta)_{M}
,
(\sigma,\tau)_{M}
and
(\beta,\gamma)_{M}
as defined above, sheaves of modules on permutohedral space form a
Cat
-valued cospecies
Mod
.

Proof.

We have four coherence
3
-cells to check. Recall
(\sigma,\tau,\beta)
-coherence says

{(\sigma\circ\tau,\beta)}_{M}\circ{\widetilde{(\sigma,\tau)}}_{M}={(\sigma,\tau)}_{\widetilde{M}}\circ(\tau,\beta)^{\prime}_{M}\circ{(\sigma,\beta)}_{M^{\prime}}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime}}
, the left-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto(g\otimes 1)\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}

and the right-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto(\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes 1)\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto(\text{g}\otimes 1)\otimes(\beta^{\ast}f_{1})\cdot 1\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto(\text{g}\otimes 1)\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}

and
\sigma^{\ast}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}=\beta^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}
by
(\sigma,\beta)
-functoriality of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
. Recall
(\beta,\gamma,\sigma)
-coherence says

(\sigma,\gamma\circ\beta)_{M}\circ(\beta,\gamma)_{M^{\prime}}=(\beta,\gamma)^{\prime}_{M}\circ(\sigma,\beta)_{\widetilde{M}}\circ\widetilde{(\sigma,\gamma)}_{M}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{\widetilde{\widetilde{F^{\prime}}}}
, the left-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto(g\otimes 1)\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\gamma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}

and the right-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto(g\otimes 1)\otimes(\gamma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto(g\otimes 1)\otimes 1\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\gamma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto(g\otimes 1)\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\gamma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}.

Recall
(\sigma,\tau,\upsilon)
-coherence says

(\sigma\circ\tau,\upsilon)_{M}\circ(\sigma,\tau)_{M^{\prime}}=(\sigma,\tau\circ\upsilon)_{M}\circ(\tau,\upsilon)^{\prime}_{M}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F^{\prime\prime\prime}}
, the left-hand map is given by

g\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}.

and the right-hand map is given by

g\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes(\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}.

Finally, recall
(\beta,\gamma,\delta)
-coherence says

(\gamma\circ\beta,\delta)_{M}\circ(\beta,\gamma)_{\widetilde{M}}=(\beta,\delta\circ\gamma)_{M}\circ\widetilde{(\gamma,\delta)}_{M}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{\widetilde{\widetilde{\widetilde{F}}}}
, the left-hand map is given by

g\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\gamma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}.

and the right-hand map is given by

g\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes(\gamma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\gamma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}.\qed

We now show that
Mod
is weakly Joyal-theoretic by taking external tensor products of modules. Let
Mod
denote the Joyalization of
Mod
. Given an
F
-tuple of modules
M=(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})\in\text{Mod}[F]
, we have the pullback modules
\pi^{\ast}_{S_{i}}M_{i}\in\textsf{Mod}_{F}
given by

\pi^{\ast}_{S_{i}}M_{i}=\pi^{-1}_{S_{i}}M_{i}\otimes_{\pi^{-1}_{S_{i}}\mathcal{O}_{S_{i}}}\mathcal{O}_{F}.

Then, we have the morphism of cospecies the external tensor product of modules, which is given by

\text{Mod}\to\textsf{Mod},\qquad(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})\mapsto M_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{k}:=\pi^{\ast}_{S_{1}}M_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\pi^{\ast}_{S_{k}}M_{k}.

This is not a strict morphism, and so we need to define
2
-cells
\sigma_{F}
and
\beta_{F}
. In particular, for each bijection
\sigma:J\to I
and
F
-tuple
M=(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})\in\text{Mod}[F]
,
F\in\Sigma[J]
, we require a map of
\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}
-modules

\sigma_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}:(M_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{k})^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M^{\prime}_{k}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F^{\prime}}
,
\sigma_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}
is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\Big(M_{i}\big(x^{\prime}|_{S_{i}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(x^{\prime})\Big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}(x)\to\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\bigg(\Big(M_{i}(x^{\prime}|_{S_{i}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{S^{\prime}_{i}}(x|_{S_{i}})\Big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}(x)\bigg)

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(\text{g}_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes\text{f}\ \mapsto\ \text{f}\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1)\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big).

For each permutation
\beta\in\text{Sym}_{k}
and
F
-tuple
M=(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})\in\text{Mod}[F]
,
l(F)=k
, we require a map of
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}
-modules

\beta_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}:\widetilde{M_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{k}}\to\widetilde{M}_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\widetilde{M}_{k}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{\widetilde{F}}
,
\beta_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}
is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\Big(M_{i}\big(\tilde{x}|_{S_{i}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(\tilde{x})\Big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}(x)\to\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\bigg(\Big(M_{i}(\tilde{x}|_{S_{i}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{S_{\beta(i)}}(x|_{S_{i}})\Big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}(x)\bigg)

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(\text{g}_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes\text{f}\ \mapsto\ \text{f}\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1)\otimes\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big).

Proposition 7.2
.

Equipped with the
2
-cells
\sigma_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}
and
\beta_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}
as defined above, the external product of modules is a lax morphism of
Cat
-valued cospecies

\text{Mod}\to\textsf{Mod}.

Proof.

There are three coherence
3
-cells to check. Recall
(\sigma,\beta)
-coherence says

\underbrace{\eta_{\widetilde{F}^{\prime}}\big((\sigma,\beta)_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}\big)}_{=\,\text{id}}\circ\beta_{(M^{\prime}_{1},\dots,M^{\prime}_{k})}\circ\widetilde{\sigma}_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}={\sigma}_{(M_{\beta(1)},\dots,M_{\beta(k)})}\circ\beta^{\prime}_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}\circ(\sigma,\beta)_{M_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{k}}.

The left-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(\text{g}_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes\text{f}\otimes\text{h}\ \mapsto\ \text{f}\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1)\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big)\otimes\text{h}\ \mapsto\ \text{h}(\beta^{\ast}\text{f})\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\otimes\beta^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big)

and the right-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(\text{g}_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes\text{f}\otimes\text{h}\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(\text{g}_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes 1\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f})\text{h}\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1)\otimes\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big)\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f})\text{h}

\mapsto\ (\beta^{\ast}\text{f})\text{h}\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big).

We have
\beta^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}=\sigma^{\ast}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}
by
(\sigma,\beta)
-functoriality of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
. Recall
\sigma
-coherence says

(\sigma\circ\tau)_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}\circ(\sigma,\tau)_{M_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{k}}=\eta_{F^{\prime\prime}}\big((\sigma,\tau)_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}\big)\circ\sigma_{(M^{\prime}_{1},\dots,M^{\prime}_{k})}\circ\tau^{\prime}_{(M_{1},\dots,M_{k})}.

The left-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(\text{g}_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes\text{h}_{1}\otimes\text{h}_{2}\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(\text{g}_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{h}_{1})\text{h}_{2}\ \mapsto\ (\sigma^{\ast}\text{h}_{1})\text{h}_{2}\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1)\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big)

and the right-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(\text{g}_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes\text{h}_{1}\otimes\text{h}_{2}\ \mapsto\ \text{h}_{1}\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1)\otimes\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big)\otimes\text{h}_{2}

\mapsto\ \text{h}_{2}(\sigma^{\ast}\text{h}_{1})\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big(((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1)\otimes 1)\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big)\ \mapsto\ \text{h}_{2}(\sigma^{\ast}\text{h}_{1})\cdot\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big((\text{g}_{i}\otimes 1)\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{i}\big).

Then
\beta
-coherence follows similarly.
∎

7.3.
The Bimonoid of Sheaves of Modules

We now give
Mod
the structure of a bimonoid by pulling back modules with respect to the (co)multiplication of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
, that is the multiplication and comultiplication of
Mod
are given by

\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M=\Updelta^{-1}_{F,G}M\otimes_{\Updelta_{F,G}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{G}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}M=\upmu^{-1}_{F,G}M\otimes_{\upmu_{F,G}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{G}}\mathcal{O}_{F}

respectively. This is not strict, so we need to define seven invertible
2
-cells

\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma},\ \Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\beta},\ \Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,E},\ \upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma},\ \upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,\beta},\ \upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,E},\ \text{B}_{F,G,A}.

As in the case of the
2
-cells for
Mod
as a cospecies, they are just the canonical isomorphisms which express how pullback of sheaves of modules is functorial. We now make them explicit.

We need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{G^{\prime}}
-modules

{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma}}_{M}:(\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M)^{\prime}\to\Updelta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}(M^{\prime}).

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G^{\prime}}
,
{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma}}_{M}
is given by

M\big(\Updelta_{F,G}(x^{\prime})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}(x^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G^{\prime}}(x)\to M\big(\Updelta_{F,G}(x^{\prime})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}\big(\Updelta_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G^{\prime}}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

We need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{G}}
-modules

{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\beta}}_{M}:\widetilde{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M}\to\Updelta^{\ast}_{\widetilde{F},\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{M}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{\widetilde{G}}
,
{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\beta}}_{M}
is given by

M\big(\Delta_{F,G}(\tilde{x})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}(\tilde{x})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{G}}(x)\to M\big(\Delta_{F,G}(\tilde{x})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}\big(\Updelta_{\widetilde{F},\widetilde{G}}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{G}}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

We need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-modules

\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,E}:\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,E}(\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M)\to\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,E}M.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{E}
,
\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,E}
is given by

M\big(\Updelta_{F,E}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}\big(\Delta_{G,E}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{E}(x)\to M\big(\Updelta_{F,E}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{E}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\Updelta_{G,E}^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

We need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}
-modules

{\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma}}_{M}:(\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}M)^{\prime}\to\upmu^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}(M^{\prime})

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F^{\prime}}
,
{\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma}}_{M}
is given by

M\big(\upmu_{F,G}(x^{\prime})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(x^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}(x)\to M\big(\upmu_{F,G}(x^{\prime})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G^{\prime}}\big(\upmu_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

We need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}
-modules

{\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,\beta}}_{M}:\widetilde{\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}M}\to\upmu^{\ast}_{\widetilde{F},\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{M}.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{\widetilde{F}}
,
{\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,\beta}}_{M}
is given by

M\big(\Delta_{F,G}(\tilde{x})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(\tilde{x})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}(x)\to M\big(\Delta_{F,G}(\tilde{x})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{G}}\big(\upmu_{\widetilde{F},\widetilde{G}}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

We need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-modules

\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,E}:\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}(\upmu^{\ast}_{G,E}M)\to\upmu^{\ast}_{F,E}M.

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
,
\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G,E}
is given by

\upmu^{\ast}:M\big(\upmu_{F,E}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}\big(\Delta_{F,G}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(x)\to M\big(\upmu_{F,E}(x)\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\upmu_{F,G}^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}.

Finally, we need a natural isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{G}
-modules

{\text{B}_{F,G,A}}_{M}:\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\upmu^{\ast}_{FG,F}M\to\upmu^{\ast}_{G,A}\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,A}M

On the stalk at
x\in\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}
,
{\text{B}_{F,G,A}}_{M}
is given by

M(\Updelta_{F,A}(\upmu_{G,A}(x)))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{FG}(\beta(\Updelta_{GF,G}(x)))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{GF}(\Updelta_{GF,G}(x))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}(x)

\to M(\Updelta_{F,A}\upmu_{G,A}(x))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{A}(\upmu_{G,A}(x))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}(x)

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto g\otimes 1\otimes(\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}.

Theorem 7.3
.

The pullback of sheaves of modules along the (co)multiplication of permutohedral space, together with
2
-cells the canonical isomorphisms which express how pullback of sheaves of modules is functorial, gives
Mod
the structure of a bimonoid in
Cat
-valued cospecies.

Proof.

There are eleven coherence laws to check. Recall associativity
\sigma
-coherence says

{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime},E^{\prime}}}_{M^{\prime}}\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{G^{\prime},E^{\prime}}\big({\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma}}_{M}\big)\circ{\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,E,\sigma}}_{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M}={\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,E,\sigma}}_{M}\circ({\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,E}}_{M})^{\prime}.

The left-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\Updelta_{G^{\prime},E^{\prime}}^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}

and the right-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\Updelta_{G,E}^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\Updelta_{G,E}^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}

and
\Updelta_{G^{\prime},E^{\prime}}^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}=\sigma^{\ast}\Updelta_{G,E}^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}
by comultiplication
\sigma
-naturality of permutohedral space
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
. Then associativity
\beta
-coherence follows similarly by comultiplication
\beta
-naturality of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
. Also, coassociativity
\sigma
-coherence
and coassociativity
\beta
-coherence
follow similarly by multiplication
\sigma
-naturality
and multiplication
\beta
-naturality
of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
. Recall
\sigma
-functoriality multiplication coherence says

\Updelta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime\prime},G^{\prime\prime}}\big((\sigma,\tau)_{M}\big)\circ{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime},\sigma}}_{M^{\prime}}\circ({\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\tau}}_{M})^{\prime}={\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma\circ\tau}}_{M}\circ(\sigma,\tau)_{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M}.

The left-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}

and the right-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}.

Then
\beta
-functoriality multiplication coherence follows similarly. Also,
\sigma
-functoriality comultiplication coherence and
\beta
-functoriality comultiplication coherence follow similarly.

Recall associativity coherence says

{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,E,D}}_{M}\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{E,D}({{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,E}}_{M}})={\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G,D}}_{M}\circ{\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,E,D}}_{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M}.

The left-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,E}\text{f}_{1})\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\Updelta^{\ast}_{E,D}\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,E}\text{f}_{1})(\Updelta^{\ast}_{E,D}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}

and the right-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes(\Updelta^{\ast}_{E,D}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes(\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,D}\text{f}_{1})(\Updelta^{\ast}_{E,G}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}

and

\Updelta^{\ast}_{E,D}\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,E}\text{f}_{1}=\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,D}\text{f}_{1}

by coassociativity of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
. Then coassociativity coherence follows similarly by associativity of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
. Finally, recall bimonoid
\sigma
-coherence says

\upmu_{G,A}({\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,A,\sigma}}_{M})\circ{\upmu_{G,A,\sigma}}_{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,A}M}\circ{\text{B}^{\prime}_{F,G,A}}_{M}

={\text{B}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime},A^{\prime}}}_{M^{\prime}}\circ\Delta^{\ast}_{GF,G}({\widetilde{\upmu_{FG,F,\sigma}}}_{M})\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\big({(\sigma,\beta)}^{-1}_{\upmu^{\ast}_{FG,F}M}\big)\circ{\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G,\sigma}}_{\widetilde{\upmu^{\ast}_{FG,F}M}}.

The left-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\otimes\text{f}_{4}\mapsto g\otimes 1\otimes(\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\text{f}_{2})\text{f}_{3}\otimes\text{f}_{4}

\mapsto g\otimes 1\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\text{f}_{2})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{3})\text{f}_{4}\mapsto g\otimes 1\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\sigma^{\ast}\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\text{f}_{2})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{3})\text{f}_{4}

and the right-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes\text{f}_{3}\otimes\text{f}_{4}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes\text{f}_{2}\otimes 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{3})\text{f}_{4}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes\text{f}_{1}\otimes 1\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2}\cdot 1\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{3})\text{f}_{4}

\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}\otimes\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2}\otimes(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{3})\text{f}_{4}\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes 1\otimes(\Updelta^{\ast}_{G^{\prime}F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}\beta^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1})(\Updelta^{\ast}_{G^{\prime}F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2})(\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{3})\text{f}_{4}.

We have

\sigma^{\ast}\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}=\Updelta^{\ast}_{G^{\prime}F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}\beta^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sigma^{\ast}\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\text{f}_{2}=\Updelta^{\ast}_{G^{\prime}F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}\sigma^{\ast}\text{f}_{2}

by multiplication
\sigma
-naturality and
(\sigma,\beta)
-functoriality of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
.
∎

7.4.
The
Mod
-Graded Bialgebra of Global Sections

Given an
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-module
M
, a
global section

s\in M
is a map of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-modules of the form

s:\mathcal{O}_{F}\to M.

We have the natural identification

\operatorname{Hom}_{\textsf{Mod}_{F}}(\mathcal{O}_{F},M)\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}(M),\qquad s\ \mapsto\ \big(x\mapsto s(1_{\mathcal{O}_{F}(x)})\big).

We have the
Mod
-graded vector cospecies
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}
given by taking global sections, pulling them back along the corresponding maps of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
, and pushing them forward along maps of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-modules,

\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}_{M}[F]:=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}(M),

\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}_{M}[\sigma,F](s):=\sigma^{\ast}s=(\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\underbrace{\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{F}}_{\text{`pullback of the structure sheaf is the structure sheaf'}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\xrightarrow{s^{\prime}}M^{\prime}),\qquad\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}_{M}[F,\beta](s):=\beta^{\ast}s=(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{F}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{F}\xrightarrow{\tilde{s}}\widetilde{M}),

\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}_{f}[F,F](s):=f(s)=f\circ s.

Here we have used the abbreviations
s^{\prime}=\textsf{Mod}[\sigma,F](s)
and
\tilde{s}=\textsf{Mod}[F,\beta](s)
. To avoid ambiguity, from now on let

\sigma^{\ast}(s):=\textsf{Mod}[\sigma,F](s)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sigma^{\ast}s:=\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}_{M}[\sigma,F](s).

Similarly with the
\beta
’s. The verification that
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}
is functorial, and so does indeed define a cospecies, is straightforward and follows along the lines of the proof of
7.4
below.

We can also pullback global sections with respect to the (co)multiplication of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
, giving maps of vector spaces

{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}}_{M}:\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}(M)\to\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}}(\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M),\qquad s\mapsto\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}s=(\mathcal{O}_{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}(s)}\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}M)

and

{\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}}_{M}:\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}}(M)\to\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}(\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}M),\qquad s\mapsto\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}s=(\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}\mathcal{O}_{G}\xrightarrow{\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}(s)}\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}M).

Proposition 7.4
.

The pullback of global sections along the (co)multiplication of permutohedral
\mathbb{C}\Sigma
space equips
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}
with the structure of a
Mod
-graded bialgebra in cospecies.

Proof.

There are eleven diagrams to check. For multiplication
\sigma
-naturality
, we need the map

\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma}\,\circ\,\sigma^{\ast}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}s=\mathcal{O}_{G^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}\mathcal{O}_{F})^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sigma^{\ast}(\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}(s))}(\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}M)^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G,\sigma}}\Delta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}M^{\prime}

to equal the map

\Delta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}\sigma^{\ast}s=\mathcal{O}_{G^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{F}\xrightarrow{\Delta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}(\sigma^{\ast}(s))}\Delta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}M^{\prime}.

The first map is given on stalks by

\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto s(1)\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto s(1)\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}

and the second map by

\text{f}\mapsto\text{f}\otimes 1\mapsto\text{f}\otimes 1\otimes 1\mapsto s(1)\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}.

Comultiplication
\sigma
-naturality and (co)multiplication
\beta
-naturality follow similarly. For multiplication
\mathtt{f}
-naturality, we have

\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}(f)\big({\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}}s\big)=\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}(f)\circ{\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}}s=\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}(f\circ s)\circ\Psi=\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}f(s)

where
\Psi
denotes the canonical isomorphism
\Psi:\mathcal{O}_{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Comultiplication
\mathtt{f}
-naturality follows similarly. For associativity, we need the map

\Delta^{\ast}_{F,E}s=\mathcal{O}_{E}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,E}\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{\Delta^{\ast}_{F,E}(s)}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,E}M

to equal the map

{\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G,E}}_{M}\circ\Delta^{\ast}_{G,E}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}s=\mathcal{O}_{E}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta^{\ast}_{G,E}\mathcal{O}_{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta^{\ast}_{G,E}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{\Delta^{\ast}_{G,E}(\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}(s))}\Delta^{\ast}_{G,E}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G}M\xrightarrow{{\Delta^{\ast}_{F,G,E}}_{M}}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,E}M.

The first map is given on stalks by

\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto s(\text{1})\otimes\text{f}

and the second map by

\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto s(1)\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto s(1)\otimes\text{f}.

Coassociativity follows similarly. (Co)unitality is clear. For the bimonoid axiom, we need the map

\mu^{\ast}_{G,A}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,A}s=\mathcal{O}_{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mu^{\ast}_{G,A}\mathcal{O}_{A}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mu^{\ast}_{G,A}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,A}\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{\mu^{\ast}_{G,A}(\Delta^{\ast}_{F,A}(s))}\mu^{\ast}_{G,A}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,A}M

to equal the map

{\text{B}_{F,G,A}}_{M}\circ\Delta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\upmu_{FG,F}s=\mathcal{O}_{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\mathcal{O}_{GF}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{FG}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\upmu^{\ast}_{FG,F}\mathcal{O}_{F}

\xrightarrow{\Delta^{\ast}_{GF,G}(\beta^{\ast}(\upmu^{\ast}_{FG,F}(s)))}\Delta^{\ast}_{GF,G}\beta^{\ast}\upmu^{\ast}_{FG,F}M\xrightarrow{{\text{B}_{F,G,A}}_{M}}\mu^{\ast}_{G,A}\Delta^{\ast}_{F,A}M.

The first map is given on stalks by

\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto s(\text{1})\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}

and the second map by

\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto 1\otimes 1\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto s(\text{1})\otimes 1\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}\mapsto s(\text{1})\otimes 1\otimes\text{f}.\qed

8.
Invertible Sheaves and Boolean Functions

We now study an indexing of sheaves of modules which has essential image the invertible sheaves. Invertible sheaves are often constructed as subsheaves of the sheaf of rational functions, and may be indexed by torus invariant Cartier divisors. However, we require a slightly different approach in order to expose the bimonoid structure of invertible sheaves.

8.1.
The Bimonoid of Boolean Functions

Let
[I,\text{2}]
denote the set of all subsets
A\subseteq I
. We have the Joyal-theoretic cospecies of
integer-valued

Boolean functions

BF
, given by

\textsf{BF}[I]:=\big\{\text{functions}\ z:[I,\text{2}]\to\mathbb{Z}\ \big|\ z(\emptyset)=0\big\},\qquad\textsf{BF}[\sigma](z)\,(A):=z(A^{\prime}).

In particular, for each integer point
h=(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}I
we have the Boolean function
z_{h}
given by

z_{h}(A):=\langle h,\lambda_{A}\rangle=\sum_{\text{i}\in A}h_{\text{i}}.

We let
\textsf{BF}[I]
be a category by including a single morphism
z_{1}\to z_{2}
whenever
z_{2}=z_{1}+z_{h}
for some
h\in\mathbb{Z}I
. This is an equivalence relation.

We call
\text{hei}(z):=z(I)
the
height
of
z
. For
z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})\in\textsf{BF}[F]
, height is a
k
-tuple

\text{hei}(z_{1},\dots,z_{k}):=\big(\text{hei}(z_{1}),\dots,\text{hei}(z_{k})\big).

We now give
BF
the structure of a bimonoid, following
[
AA17
]
. Given Boolean functions
z_{1}\in\textsf{BF}[S]
and
z_{2}\in\textsf{BF}[T]
, let
(z_{1}|z_{2})\in\textsf{BF}[I]
be the Boolean function given by

(z_{1}|z_{2})(A):=z_{1}(A\cap S)+z_{2}(A\cap T).

Given a Boolean function
z\in\textsf{BF}[I]
, let
z\talloblong_{S}\in\textsf{BF}[S]
and
z\!\!\fatslash_{\,T}\in\textsf{BF}[T]
be the Boolean functions given by

z\!\talloblong_{S}(A):=z(A)\qquad\text{and}\qquad z\!\!\!\fatslash_{\,T}(A):=z(A\sqcup S)-z(S).

Then
BF
is a Joyal-theoretic bimonoid in
Cat
-valued cospecies, with multiplication and comultiplication given by

\mu_{S,T}(z_{1},z_{2}):=(z_{1}|z_{2})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Delta_{S,T}(z):=(z\talloblong_{S},z\!\!\fatslash_{\,T})

respectively. For the higher comultiplication, we denote e.g.

(z\talloblong_{S},z\talloblong_{T},z\talloblong_{U}):=\Delta_{(S,T,U)}(z).

8.2.
Invertible Sheaves From Cartier Divisors

We also have the Joyal-theoretic cospecies
\mathbb{Z}^{[-;\text{2}]}
, given by

\mathbb{Z}^{[I;\text{2}]}:=\big\{\text{functions}\ w:[I;\text{2}]\to\mathbb{Z}\big\},\qquad\sigma(w)\big((S,T)\big):=w\big((S^{\prime},T^{\prime})\big).

The functions
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{[I;\text{2}]}
are in one-to-one correspondence with torus invariant Cartier divisors
D_{w}
on permutohedral space
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
. Recall the invertible sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{w}\in\text{Mod}_{I}
associated to
D_{w}
is the submodule of the sheaf of rational functions given by

\mathcal{O}_{w}(U):=\big\{g\in\mathcal{K}_{I}\ \big|\ g=0\text{ or }\text{div}g+D_{w}|_{U}\geq 0\big\}.

See e.g.
[
CLS11
, Chapter 6]
. The essential image of the indexing
w\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{w}
is all the invertible sheaves.

We have the inclusion of height zero Boolean functions

\mathbb{Z}^{[-;2]}\hookrightarrow\textsf{BF},\qquad w\,\mapsto\,\begin{cases}\big(A\mapsto w(A,B)\big),\quad\text{for $A\neq I,\emptyset$}\\
(I\mapsto 0)\\
(\emptyset\mapsto 0)\end{cases}

where
B=I-A
. The idea is to now generalize this construction of invertible sheaves to all of
BF
.

8.3.
Affine Semisimple Flats

We give a geometric interpretation of the algebraic structure of
BF
, analogous to what we found for augmented preposets
\textsf{O}_{\bullet}
. First, we need an analog of the monoid
\text{T}^{\vee}
.

For
a\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,a}
denote the ‘sum equal to
a
’ hyperplane of
\mathbb{R}I
, given by

\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,a}=\Big\{h\in\mathbb{R}I\ \Big|\ \sum_{\text{i}\in I}h_{\text{i}}=a\Big\}.

For
F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{k})
a composition of
I
and a
k
-tuple of integers
