(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, we have the affine
semisimple flat

\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}
, given by

\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}:=\Big\{h\in\mathbb{R}I\ \Big|\ \sum_{\text{i}\in S_{i}}h_{\text{i}}=a_{i}\quad\text{for all}\quad 1\leq i\leq k\Big\}.

A key fact is that semisimple flats factorize; we have the bijective map
\eta_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}
given by

\eta_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}:\text{T}^{\vee}_{S_{1},a_{1}}\times\dots\times\text{T}^{\vee}_{S_{k},a_{k}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})},\qquad\big((h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in S_{1}},\dots,(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in S_{k}}\big)\mapsto(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in I}.

For compositions
F,G
with
G\leq F
, let

F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{k})\qquad\text{and}\qquad G=(T_{1},\dots,T_{l})

and let
1\leq\mathtt{i}_{j}\leq k
be the integer such that
S_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}
is the
\mathtt{i}
th lump of
F|_{T_{j}}
,
1\leq j\leq l
. Let
k(j)
be the length of
F|_{T_{j}}
. Then we have the function
{\mu_{F,G}}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}
the inclusion of semisimple flats,

{\mu_{F,G}}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}:\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}\hookrightarrow\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{G,(a_{1_{1}}+\,\cdots\,+a_{k(1)_{1}}\,,\dots\dots,\,a_{1_{l}}+\,\cdots\,+a_{k(l)_{l}})},\qquad h\mapsto h.

We have the function the composition
\text{m}_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}:={\mu_{F,(I)}}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}\circ{\eta_{F}}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}
, which is given by

\text{m}_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}:\text{T}^{\vee}_{S_{1},a_{1}}\times\dots\times\text{T}^{\vee}_{S_{k},a_{k}}\to\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,a_{1}+\dots+a_{k}},\qquad\big((h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in S_{1}},\dots,(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in S_{k}}\big)\mapsto(h_{\text{i}})_{\text{i}\in I}.

Remark 8.1
.

In fact,
(F,a)\mapsto\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,a}
defines a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded set cospecies, where
\mathbb{Z}
denotes the constant cospecies
F\mapsto\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover, this cospecies is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded monoid, with multiplication the inclusion of semisimple flats. Then
\text{m}_{F,(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}
is the multiplication
{\mu_{F,(I)}}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})}
of its Joyalization.

8.4.
Plate Arrangements

To each Boolean function
z\in\textsf{BF}[I]
we associate the halfspace arrangement in
\text{T}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}^{\vee}
which consists of the halfspaces

\displaystyle[[A,B]]_{z}:=

\displaystyle\ \big\{h\in\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}\ \big|\ \langle h,\lambda_{A}\rangle\leq z(A)\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \big\{h\in\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}\ \big|\ \langle h,\lambda_{B}\rangle\geq z(I)-z(A)\big\}

where
(A,B)\in[I;2]
. More generally, we have the associated plate arrangement, which consists of the
plates

[[H]]_{z}:=\bigcap_{(A,B)\leq H}[[A,B]]_{z}

=\ \big\{h\in\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}\ \big|\ \langle h,\lambda_{\overline{A}_{j}}\rangle\leq z\big(\overline{A}_{j}\big),\quad 0<j<\ell\big\}

=\ \big\{h\in\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}\ \big|\ \langle h,\lambda_{\overline{A}_{j}}\rangle\leq z\big(\overline{A}_{j}\big),\quad 0\leq j\leq\ell\big\}

where
H=(A_{1},\dots,A_{\ell})\in\Sigma[I]
and

\overline{A}_{j}:=A_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup A_{j},\qquad 0\leq j\leq\ell.

Plates were introduced in
[
Ocn18
]
, and are studied in
[
Ear17
]
,
[
AS22
]
. A set of the form
\overline{A}_{j}
,
0\leq j\leq\ell
, is called an
initial segment
of
H
. In particular, we have
\overline{A}_{0}=\emptyset
and
\overline{A}_{\ell}=I
. Thus, as a region
[[H]]_{z}\subset\mathbb{R}I
, the plate is given by the inequalities

(12)

\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{A}_{j}}\rangle\leq z\big(\overline{A}_{j}\big)

for
0<j<\ell
, together with the equality for the ambient hyperplane

(13)

\langle h,\lambda_{I}\rangle=z(I).

If we let
j=0
in
(
12
)
we obtain the trivial inequality

0=\langle h,\lambda_{\emptyset}\rangle\leq z(\emptyset)=0

and if we let
j=\ell
in
(
12
)
we obtain

\langle h,\lambda_{I}\rangle\leq z(I)

which is implied by
(
13
)
.

The plate
[[H]]_{z}
is a module of the monoid
\sigma_{H}^{o}
, with the action given by addition of vectors in
\mathbb{R}I
. If
z_{2}=z_{1}+z_{h}
, we have the bijection

[[H]]_{z_{1}}\xrightarrow{\sim}[[H]]_{z_{2}},\qquad(-)\,\mapsto\,(-)+h.

Proposition 8.1
.

The maximum affine subspace of the plate
[[H]]_{z}
is the semisimple flat

\text{max aff}\big([[H]]_{z}\big)=\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{H,\text{hei}(\Delta_{H}(z))}.

Proof.

The maximum affine subspace of the plate
[[H]]_{z}
is obtained by replacing inequalities with equalities in
(
12
)
,

\text{max aff}\big([[H]]_{z}\big)=\big\{h\in\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}\ \big|\ \langle h,\lambda_{\overline{A}_{j}}\rangle=z\big(\overline{A}_{j}\big),\quad 0<j<\ell\big\}.

Then notice this is just

=\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{H,(z({A}_{1}),z(\overline{{A}}_{2}-\overline{{A}}_{1}),\dots,z(\overline{{A}}_{\ell-1}-\overline{{A}}_{\ell-2}),z(I-\overline{{A}}_{\ell-1}))}=\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{H,\text{hei}(\Delta_{H}(z))}.\qed

Proposition 8.2
.

For
F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{k})
a composition of
I
, given an
F
-tuple of Boolean functions
z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})\in\textsf{BF}[F]
and a composition
H\in\Sigma[I]
, the multiplication
\text{m}_{F,\text{hei}(z)}
restricts to an embedding

\text{m}_{F,\text{hei}(z)}:[[H|_{S_{1}}]]_{z_{1}}\times\dots\times[[H|_{S_{k}}]]_{z_{k}}\hookrightarrow[[H]]_{(z_{1}|\dots|z_{k})}

with image given by

\bigcap_{(A,B)\leq(H|_{S_{1}}|\dots|H|_{S_{k}})}[[A,B]]_{(z_{1}|\dots|z_{k})}.

Proof.

Let
H=(A_{1},\dots,A_{\ell})
, and let

A_{j,i}=S_{i}\cap A_{j},\qquad\overline{A}_{j,i}=A_{1,i}\sqcup\dots\sqcup A_{j,i}.

We have

H|_{S_{i}}=(A_{1,i},\dots,A_{\ell,i})_{+}.

The plate
[[H|_{S_{i}}]]_{z_{i}}\subset\mathbb{R}S_{i}
is the region satisfying the inequalities

(14)

\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{A}_{j,i}}\rangle\leq z_{i}(\overline{A}_{j,i})

for
0<j<\ell
, together with the equality for the ambient space

(15)

\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle=z_{i}(S_{i}).

The product
[[H|_{S_{1}}]]_{z_{1}}\times\dots\times[[H|_{S_{k}}]]_{z_{k}}
is the region satisfying these equalities for all
1\leq i\leq k
. On the other hand, the plate
\bigcap_{(A,B)\leq(H|_{S_{1}}|\dots|H|_{S_{k}})}[[A,B]]_{(z_{1}|\dots|z_{k})}\subset\mathbb{R}I
is the region satisfying the inequalities

(16)

\langle h,\lambda_{A}\rangle\leq(z_{1}|\dots|z_{k})(A)=z_{1}(A\cap S_{1})+\dots+z_{k}(A\cap S_{k})

where
(A,B)\leq(H|_{S_{1}}|\dots|H|_{S_{k}})
, together with the equality for the ambient space

(17)

\langle h,\lambda_{I}\rangle=(z_{1}|\dots|z_{k})(I)=z_{1}(S_{1})+\dots+z_{k}(S_{k}).

Let
\mathcal{A}_{i}:=A\cap S_{i}
, so that
A=\mathcal{A}_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\mathcal{A}_{k}
where
\mathcal{A}_{i}
is an initial segment of
H|_{S_{i}}
. Then
(
14
)
&
(
15
)
\implies
(
16
)
since

\langle h,\lambda_{A}\rangle=\langle h,\lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\rangle+\dots+\langle h,\lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}\rangle\leq z_{1}(\mathcal{A}_{1})+\dots+z_{k}(\mathcal{A}_{k}).

Clearly
(
15
)
\implies
(
17
)
. We have
(
16
)
\implies
(
14
)
by setting
A=\overline{A}_{j,i}
in
(
16
)
. We prove
(
16
)
&
(
17
)

\implies
(
15
)
in two parts. First, set
A=S_{i}
in
(
16
)
to give

\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle\leq z_{i}(S_{i}).

Second, set
A=I-S_{i}
in
(
16
)
to give
15
15

15

where the hat
\hat{z}_{i}(S_{i})
means the term
z_{i}(S_{i})
is
not
included in the sum

(18)

\langle h,\lambda_{I-S_{i}}\rangle\leq z_{1}(S_{1})+\dots+\hat{z}_{i}(S_{i})+\dots+z_{k}(S_{k}).

Then

\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle=\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\overbrace{\phantom{\langle h,\lambda_{I}\rangle-\langle h,\lambda_{I-S_{i}}\rangle=z_{1}(S_{1})+\dots+z_{k}(S_{k})-\langle h,\lambda_{I-S_{i}}\rangle}}^{\text{by {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:4})}}}$\hss}\langle h,\lambda_{I}\rangle-\langle h,\lambda_{I-S_{i}}\rangle=\underbrace{z_{1}(S_{1})+\dots+z_{k}(S_{k})-\langle h,\lambda_{I-S_{i}}\rangle\geq z_{i}(S_{i})}_{\text{by {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:5})}}}.

Thus, we have shown
\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle\leq z_{i}(S_{i})
and
\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle\geq z_{i}(S_{i})
, and so
\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle=z_{i}(S_{i})
. This completes the proof of the image.

Finally, we need to show the image is contained in
[[H]]_{(z_{1}|\dots|z_{k})}
. We have

H\leq(H|_{S_{1}}|\dots|H|_{S_{k}})

and so

(A,B)\leq H\quad\implies\quad(A,B)\leq(H|_{S_{1}}|\dots|H|_{S_{k}}).

Therefore

\bigcap_{(A,B)\leq(H|_{S_{1}}|\dots|H|_{S_{k}})}[[A,B]]_{(z_{1}|\dots|z_{k})}\ \subseteq\ [[H]]_{(z_{1}|\dots|z_{k})}.\qed

Given
(S,T)\in[I;2]
, the
(S,T)
-
face
of the plate
[[H]]_{z}
is the region of
[[H]]_{z}
on which the function
\lambda_{S}
is maximized. Given
F\in\Sigma[I]
, the
F
-
face
of
[[H]]_{z}
is the intersection of all the
(S,T)
-faces for
(S,T)\leq F
, i.e. it is the region on which
\lambda_{S}
is maximized for all
(S,T)\leq F
.

Proposition 8.3
.

If
F\leq H
, then the
F
-face of the plate
[[H]]_{z}
is given by the intersection

[[H]]_{z}\cap\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,\text{hei}(\Delta_{F}(z))}.

If
F\nleq H
, then
[[H]]_{z}
does not have an
F
-face.

Proof.

Let

H=(A_{1},\dots,A_{\ell}).

First consider the case
F=(S,T)
. If
(S,T)\leq H
, then
\lambda_{S}\leq z(S)
on
[[H]]_{z}
. But
\lambda_{S}=z(S)
on
\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{(S,T),\text{hei}(\Delta_{S,T}(z))}
by definition, and
[[H]]_{z}\cap\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{(S,T),\text{hei}(\Delta_{S,T}(z))}
is nonempty because
\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{(S,T),\text{hei}(\Delta_{S,T}(z))}
contains the maximal affine subspace of
[[H]]_{z}
.

For generic
F
, the result then follows since

\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,\text{hei}(\Delta_{F}(z))}=\text{max aff}\big([[F]]_{z}\big)=\text{max aff}\big(\bigcap_{(S,T)\leq F}[[S,T]]_{z}\big)

=\bigcap_{(S,T)\leq F}\text{max aff}\big([[S,T]]_{z}\big)=\bigcap_{(S,T)\leq F}\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{(S,T),\text{hei}(\Delta_{S,T}(z))}.

If
(S,T)\nleq H
, then
(t,s)\in H
for some
s\in S
and
t\in T
. Therefore
h_{st}\in\sigma^{o}_{H}
, and so
\lambda_{S}
obtains arbitrary large values on the
\sigma^{o}_{H}
-module
[[H]]_{z}
. If
F\nleq H
, then there exists
(S,T)\leq F
with
(S,T)\nleq H
.
∎

Proposition 8.4
.

For
F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{k})
a composition of
I
, given an
F
-tuple of compositions
K=(K_{1},\dots,K_{k})\in\mathsf{\Sigma}[F]
and a Boolean function
z\in\textsf{BF}[I]
, the function
\text{m}_{F,\text{hei}(\Delta_{F}(z))}
restricts to an embedding of cones

[[K_{1}]]_{z\talloblong_{S_{1}}}\times\dots\times[[K_{k}]]_{z\talloblong_{S_{k}}}\hookrightarrow[[K_{1};\dots;K_{k}]]_{z}

with image the
F
-face of
[[K_{1};\dots;K_{k}]]_{z}
.

Proof.

In light of
8.3
, this amount to showing that the image is given by the intersection

[[K_{1};\cdots;K_{k}]]_{z}\cap\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,\text{hei}(\Delta_{F}(z))}.

Let

K_{i}=(B_{1,i},\dots,B_{l(i),i})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{B}_{j,i}:=B_{1,i}\sqcup\dots\sqcup B_{j,i}

for
1\leq j\leq l(i)
. Then the intersection
[[K_{1};\cdots;K_{k}]]_{z}\cap\textsf{T}^{\vee}_{F,\text{hei}(\Delta_{F}(z))}\subset\mathbb{R}I
is the region given by the plate inequalities

(19)

\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i}}\rangle\leq z(\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i}),\qquad 0\leq j<l(i)

for
1\leq i\leq k
(although the equality for
i=1
,
j=0
is trivial), together with the equalities

(20)

\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle=\text{hei}(z\talloblong_{S_{i}})=z(\overline{S}_{i})-z(\overline{S}_{i-1})\qquad\text{for}\quad 1\leq i\leq k.

On the other hand,
[[K_{i}]]_{z\talloblong_{S_{i}}}\subset\mathbb{R}S_{i}
is the region given by the inequalities

(21)

\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{B}_{j,i}}\rangle\leq z\!\talloblong_{S_{i}}(\overline{B}_{j,i})=z(\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i})-z(\overline{S}_{i-1})

for
0<j<l(i)
, together with the equality for the ambient space

(22)

\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i}}\rangle=z\talloblong_{S_{i}}(S_{i})=z(\overline{S}_{i})-z(\overline{S}_{i-1}).

The product
[[K_{1}]]_{z\talloblong_{S_{1}}}\times\dots\times[[K_{k}]]_{z\talloblong_{S_{k}}}
is the region satisfying these equalities for all
1\leq i\leq k
.

Clearly
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:33})}

\iff

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:66})}
. For
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:11})}
&
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:33})}

\implies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:55})}
, we have

z(\overline{S}_{i-1})+\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{B}_{j,i}}\rangle=\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{S}_{i-1}}\rangle+\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{B}_{j,i}}\rangle=\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i}}\rangle\leq z(\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i})

and so

\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{B}_{j,i}}\rangle\leq z(\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i})-z(\overline{S}_{i-1}).

For
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:55})}
&
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:66})}

\implies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\ref{eq:11})}
, we have

\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i}}\rangle=\langle h,\lambda_{S_{1}}\rangle+\dots+\langle h,\lambda_{S_{i-1}}\rangle+\langle h,\lambda_{\overline{B}_{j,i}}\rangle

\leq z(\overline{S}_{1})+\big(z(\overline{S}_{2})-z(\overline{S}_{1})\big)+\cdots\cdots+\big(z(\overline{S}_{i-1})-z(\overline{S}_{i-2})\big)+z(\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i})-z(\overline{S}_{i-1})

=z(\overline{S}_{i-1}\sqcup\overline{B}_{j,i}).\qed

8.5.
Invertible Sheaves From Boolean Functions

We now define a morphism of cospecies

\mathcal{O}:\textsf{BF}\to\text{Mod},\qquad z\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{z}

whose composition with the external product morphism
\text{Mod}\to\textsf{Mod}
is a homomorphism of bimonoids
\varphi:\textsf{BF}\to\textsf{Mod}
. The morphism
\mathcal{O}
will be Joyal-theoretic, but not strict.

Given a composition
H\in\Sigma[I]
and a Boolean function
z\in\textsf{BF}[I]
, we have the
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]
-module
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}]
given by

\text{M}_{H,z}:=[[H]]_{z}\cap\mathbb{Z}I\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}]:=\bigoplus_{h\in\text{M}_{H,z}}\mathbb{C}\cdot g^{h}.

We define the invertible sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{z}
of
z
to be the coherent sheaf which, restricted to the affine open
U_{H}\subset\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
, is the coherent sheaf of the
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]
-module
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}]
. We now make this explicit.

We make the identification

\mathcal{K}_{I}=\text{Frac}\big(\mathbb{C}[\text{T}^{\vee}_{I}\cap\mathbb{Z}I]\big)

where recall
\mathcal{K}_{I}
denotes the field of rational functions on permutohedral space
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
. For each height
a\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have the
\mathcal{K}_{I}
-module
\mathcal{K}_{I,a}
given by

\mathcal{K}_{I,a}:=\text{Frac}\big(\mathbb{C}[\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,a}\cap\mathbb{Z}I]\big)=\mathbb{C}[\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,a}\cap\mathbb{Z}I]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\text{T}^{\vee}_{I}\cap\mathbb{Z}I]}\mathcal{K}_{I}.

Remark 8.2
.

We can think of
\mathcal{K}_{I,a}
as rational functions on permutohedral space which have an ambient degree of divergence equal to
a
. Importantly for the bimonoid structure, this extended notion of rational functions will be closed under pullback along the multiplication
\upmu_{F,G}
.

For
g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}]\subseteq\mathbb{C}[\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}\cap\mathbb{Z}I]
and
f\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]
, we abbreviate

g/f:=g\otimes 1/f\in\mathcal{K}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}.

For the principal open set
U_{f}\subseteq U_{H}
, put

(23)

\mathcal{O}_{z}(U_{f}):=\big\{g/f^{p}\ \big|\ g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}],\ p\in\mathbb{N}\big\}\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}.

Assuming this is well-defined, this defines the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{z}
on a basis of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
, and so defines the sheaf entirely. The stalk of
\mathcal{O}_{z}
at
x\in U_{H}
is given by

(24)

\mathcal{O}_{z}(x)=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\scriptscriptstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{x\in U}\mathcal{O}_{z}(U)=\big\{g/f\in\mathcal{K}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}\ \big|\ g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}],\ f(x)\neq 0\big\}.

We need to check that
(
23
)
is well-defined, and that
(
24
)
really are the stalks.

We show this by constructing isomorphisms with the subsheaves
\mathcal{O}_{w}
of the sheaf of rational functions as follows. Suppose we have principal opens

U_{f_{1}}\subseteq U_{H_{1}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad U_{f_{2}}\subseteq U_{H_{2}}\qquad\text{with}\qquad U_{f_{1}}=U_{f_{2}}=U.

Pick some
d\in\text{T}^{\vee}_{I,-\text{hei}(z)}\cap\mathbb{Z}I
. Then we have an isomorphism of vector spaces
\Phi_{\text{hei}(z),d}
given by

\Phi_{\text{hei}(z),d}:\mathcal{K}_{I,\text{hei}(z)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{K}_{I,\text{hei}(z)-\text{hei}(z)}=\mathcal{K}_{I,0}=\mathcal{K}_{I},\qquad g^{h}\otimes 1/f\ \mapsto\ g^{h+d}\otimes 1/f.

Let
w=z+z_{d}
, which is a height zero Boolean function with associated sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{w}
described in
Section 8.2
. Given
f\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]
, for the principal open
U_{f}\subseteq U_{H}
we have

\mathcal{O}_{w}(U_{f})=\big\{g/f^{p}\in\mathcal{K}_{I}\ \big|\ g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,w}],\ p\in\mathbb{N}\big\}.

See e.g. toric section 4.3. Thus

\Phi_{\text{hei}(z),d}(\mathcal{O}_{z}(U_{f_{1}}))=\mathcal{O}_{w}(U_{f_{1}})=\mathcal{O}_{w}(U)=\mathcal{O}_{w}(U_{f_{2}})=\Phi_{\text{hei}(z),d}(\mathcal{O}_{z}(U_{f_{2}})).

Since
\Phi_{\text{hei}(z),d}
is an isomorphism, we must have

\mathcal{O}_{z}(U_{f_{1}})=\mathcal{O}_{z}(U_{f_{2}})

as required. More generally, for any
d\in\mathbb{Z}I
, we have an isomorphism of invertible sheaves

\mathcal{O}_{z\to z+z_{d}}:\mathcal{O}_{z}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}_{z+z_{d}},\qquad g^{h}\otimes 1/f\ \mapsto\ g^{h+d}\otimes 1/f.

Finally, we need to define a
2
-cell
\sigma_{I}
and then check a single coherence law. In particular, we require a natural map of invertible sheaves
\sigma_{z}
of the form

\sigma_{z}:\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{z}\to\mathcal{O}_{z^{\prime}}

for each Boolean function
z\in\textsf{BF}[J]
and bijection
\sigma:J\to I
. Let
H\in\Sigma[J]
. Over
U_{H^{\prime}}\subset\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{I}
, the pullback
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{z}
is (naturally isomorphic to) the coherent sheaf of the
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}]
-module

\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}]:=\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]}\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}].

Over
U_{H^{\prime}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{z^{\prime}}
is by definition the coherent sheaf of the
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}]
-module
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime},z^{\prime}}]
. Over
U_{H^{\prime}}
, we define
\sigma_{z}
to be the isomorphism of coherent sheaves corresponding to the isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}]
-modules

\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}]\to\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime},z^{\prime}}],\qquad g\otimes f\mapsto f\cdot g^{\prime}.

Every element of the left-hand side is of the form
g\otimes 1
, and so this map is also given by
g\otimes 1\mapsto g^{\prime}
. We now make
\sigma_{z}
explicit at the level of stalks. The stalk of the pullback
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{z}
at
x\in U_{H^{\prime}}
is given by

\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{z}(x)=\mathcal{O}_{z}(x^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{I}(x)

=\big\{g/f\in\mathcal{K}_{J,\text{hei}(z)}\,\big|\,g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H,z}],\,f\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}],\,f(x^{\prime})\neq 0\big\}\otimes\big\{f_{1}/f_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{I}\,\big|\,f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}],\,f_{1}(x)\neq 0\big\}.

The stalk of
\mathcal{O}_{z^{\prime}}
at
x\in U_{H^{\prime}}
is given by

\mathcal{O}_{z^{\prime}}(x)=\big\{g/f\in\mathcal{K}_{I,\text{hei}(z^{\prime})}\,\big|\,g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime},z^{\prime}}],\,f\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H^{\prime}}],\,f(x)\neq 0\big\}.

Then the map
\sigma_{z}
on the stalk at
x\in U_{H^{\prime}}
is given by

g/f\otimes f_{1}/f_{2}\,\mapsto\,(f_{1}\cdot g^{\prime})/(f_{2}\cdot f^{\prime}).

This gives a globally well-defined
\sigma_{I}
as we vary the affine chart
U_{H^{\prime}}
by
(\sigma,\mathtt{f})
-functoriality of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
. We write germs as e.g.
\text{g}=g/f
and
\text{g}^{\prime}=g^{\prime}/f^{\prime}
. Every element of
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{z}(x)
is of the form
\text{g}\otimes 1
. Then this map may be written as

\text{g}\otimes 1\mapsto\text{g}^{\prime}.

Proposition 8.5
.

We have a Joyal-theoretic morphism of cospecies

\mathcal{O}:\textsf{BF}\to\text{Mod}.

This is a strong morphism.

Proof.

Recall
\sigma
-coherence (for cospecies) says

(\sigma\circ\tau)_{z}\circ(\sigma,\tau)_{\mathcal{O}_{z}}=\underbrace{\eta_{F^{\prime\prime}}\big((\sigma,\tau)_{z}\big)}_{=\,\text{id}}\circ\,\sigma_{z^{\prime}}\circ\tau^{\prime}_{z}.

At each stalk, the left-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes 1\mapsto\text{g}\otimes 1\mapsto\text{g}^{\prime\prime}

and right-hand map is given by

\text{g}\otimes 1\otimes 1\mapsto\text{g}^{\prime}\otimes 1\mapsto(\text{g}^{\prime})^{\prime}=\text{g}^{\prime\prime}.\qed

Remark 8.3
.

The restriction of the morphism
\textsf{BF}\to\text{Mod}
to height zero Boolean functions
\mathbb{Z}^{[-;2]}\hookrightarrow\textsf{BF}
recovers the usual construction of invertible sheaves on toric varieties as subsheaves of the sheaf of rational functions. Thus, the essential image of
z\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{z}
is all invertible sheaves.

8.6.
A Homomorphism of Bimonoids

We have the composition of morphisms
\varphi:=\text{prod}\circ\mathcal{O}
,

\varphi:\textsf{BF}\to\textsf{Mod},\qquad(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{k}}.

We now equip
\varphi
with the structure of a strong homomorphism of bimonoids, which involves defining
2
-cells
{\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}
and
{\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,G}}
and then checking nine coherence laws.

For compositions
G\leq F
, let

F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{k})\qquad\text{and}\qquad G=(T_{1},\dots,T_{l}).

Let
1\leq\mathtt{i}_{j}\leq k
be the integer such that
S_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}
is the
\mathtt{i}
th lump of
F|_{T_{j}}
,
1\leq j\leq l
. Let
k(j)
denote the length of
F|_{T_{j}}
.

Given an
F
-tuple of Boolean functions
z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})\in\textsf{BF}[F]
, we require a map of
\mathcal{O}_{G}
-modules
{\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}
of the form

{\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}:\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{k}})\to\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1_{1}}|\dots|z_{k(1)_{1}})}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1_{l}}|\dots|z_{k(l)_{l}})}.

Let
H=(H_{1},\dots,H_{l})\in\mathsf{\Sigma}[G]
. Over
U_{H}\subset\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{G}
, the pullback
\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}=\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{k}})
is (naturally isomorphic to) the coherent sheaf of the
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]
-module

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H|_{S_{i}},z_{i}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(H|_{S_{1}},\dots,H|_{S_{k}})}]\Big)\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]\,:=

{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}}_{\,\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(H|_{S_{1}},\dots,H|_{S_{k}})}]}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H|_{S_{i}},z_{i}}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H|_{S_{i}}}]}\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(H|_{S_{1}},\dots,H|_{S_{k}})}]\Big)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(H|_{S_{1}},\dots,H|_{S_{k}})}]}\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}].

The external product
\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1_{1}}|\dots|z_{k(1)_{1}})}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1_{l}}|\dots|z_{k(l)_{l}})}
over
U_{H}
is (naturally isomorphic to) the coherent sheaf of the
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]
-module

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{j},(z_{1_{j}}|\dots|z_{k(j)_{j}})}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]\Big):={\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}}_{\,\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]}\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{j},(z_{1_{j}}|\dots|z_{k(j)_{j}})}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{j}}]}\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}].

Over
U_{H}
, we define
{\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}
to be the isomorphism of coherent sheaves which corresponds to the isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]
-modules

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H|_{S_{i}},z_{i}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(H|_{S_{1}},\dots,H|_{S_{k}})}]\Big)\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]\to\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{j},(z_{1_{j}}|\dots|z_{k(j)_{j}})}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]\Big)

\underbrace{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(g^{h_{i}}\otimes 1)\otimes 1=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\bigotimes_{1_{j}\leq\mathtt{i}\leq k(j)_{j}}\big(g^{h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}}\otimes 1\big)\Big)\otimes 1}_{\text{collect into lumps of $G$}}\,\mapsto\,\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}g^{\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}(\bigtimes_{1_{j}\leq\mathtt{i}\leq k(j)_{j}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}})}\otimes 1

where
a_{j}=(\text{hei}(z_{1_{j}}),\dots,\text{hei}(z_{k(j)_{j}}))
. Note not every element of the left-hand side is of the form

\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}]
-linearly
. This map of modules is well-defined by
8.2
. It is an isomorphism because the map of

We now make
{\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}
explicit at the level of stalks. The stalk of

at

\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}(x)=\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\Big(\mathcal{O}_{z_{i}}(x|_{S_{i}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\big(\Updelta_{F,G}(x)\big)\Big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}(x)

=\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\Big(\big\{g/f\in\mathcal{K}_{S_{i},\text{hei}(z_{i})}\ \big|\ g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H|_{S_{i}},z_{i}}],\,f(x|_{S_{i}})\neq 0\big\}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\big(\Updelta_{F,G}(x)\big)\Big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}(x).

The stalk of
\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1_{1}}|\dots|z_{k(1)_{1}})}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1_{l}}|\dots|z_{k(l)_{l}})}
at
x\in U_{H}
is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\big(\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1_{j}}|\dots|z_{k(j)_{j}})}(x|_{T_{j}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}(x)\big)

=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\big\{g/f\in\mathcal{K}_{T_{j},\text{hei}(z_{1_{j}}|\dots|z_{k(j)_{j}})}\ \big|\ g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H|_{j},(z_{1_{j}}|\dots|z_{k(j)_{j}})}],\,f(x|_{T_{j}})\neq 0\big\}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}(x)\Big).

Then the map of stalks is given by extending the assignment

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(g^{h_{i}}\otimes 1)\otimes 1\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\big(g^{\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}(\bigtimes_{1_{j}\leq\mathtt{i}_{j}\leq k(j)_{j}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}})}\otimes 1\big)

\mathcal{O}_{G}(x)
-linearly
.
16
16

16

explicitly,
\bigotimes_{i}(g^{h_{i}}/f_{i}\otimes\text{f}_{i})\otimes\text{f}\ \mapsto\ {\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$(\prod_{i}\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}\text{f}_{i})\text{f}$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\prod_{i}(\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}\pi^{\ast}_{S_{i}}f_{i})$}\right.}\bigotimes_{j}\big(g^{\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}})}\otimes 1\big)
This gives a globally well-defined
{\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}
as we vary the affine chart
U_{H}
by multiplication
\mathtt{f}
-naturality of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
. When checking the coherence laws for a homomorphism of bimonoids in

, we shall abbreviate e.g.
h=(g^{h}\otimes 1)\otimes 1
, so that the map gets written as

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h_{i}\,\mapsto\,\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\Big).

Given a
G
-tuple of Boolean functions
z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{l})\in\textsf{BF}[G]
, we require a map of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-modules
{\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{l})}
of the form

{\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{l})}:\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{l}})\to\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}\talloblong_{S_{1}}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{l}\talloblong_{S_{k}}}.

Let
K=(K_{1},\dots,K_{k})\in\mathsf{\Sigma}[F]
. Over
U_{K}\subset\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
, the pullback
\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{l}})
is (naturality isomorphic to) the coherent sheaf of the
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]
-module

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{1_{j}};\cdots;K_{k(j)_{j}},z_{j}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(K_{1_{1}};\cdots;K_{k(1)_{1}},\cdots,K_{1_{l}};\cdots;K_{k(l)_{l}})}]\Big)\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]:=

{\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}}_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(K_{1_{1}};\cdots;K_{k(1)_{1}},\cdots,K_{1_{l}};\cdots;K_{k(l)_{l}})}]}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{1_{j}};\cdots;K_{k(j)_{j}},z_{j}}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[M_{K_{1_{j}};\cdots;K_{k(j)_{j}}}]}\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(K_{1_{1}};\cdots;K_{k(1)_{1}},\cdots,K_{1_{l}};\cdots;K_{k(l)_{l}})}]\Big)

\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(K_{1_{1}};\cdots;K_{k(1)_{1}},\cdots,K_{1_{l}};\cdots;K_{k(l)_{l}})}]}\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}].

Over
U_{K}
, the external product
\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}\talloblong_{S_{1}}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{l}\talloblong_{S_{k}}}
is (naturality isomorphic to) the coherent sheaf of the
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]
-module

{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{i},z\talloblong_{S_{i}}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]\Big):={\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}}_{\,\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{i},z\talloblong_{S_{i}}}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{i}}]}\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]\Big).

Over
U_{K}
, we define
{\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{l})}
to be the isomorphism of coherent sheaves which corresponds to the isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]
-modules

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{1_{j}};\cdots;K_{k(j)_{j}},z_{j}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{(K_{1_{1}};\cdots;K_{k(1)_{1}},\cdots,K_{1_{l}};\cdots;K_{k(l)_{l}})}]\Big)\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]\to{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}}\Big(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{i},z\talloblong_{S_{i}}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K}]\Big)

\underbrace{\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}(g^{h_{j}}\otimes 1)\otimes 1=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\big(g^{\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}(\bigtimes_{1_{j}\leq\mathtt{i}\leq k(j)_{j}}d_{\mathtt{i}_{j}})}\otimes 1\big)\otimes 1}_{\text{factorize each $h_{j}$}}\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\big(\bigotimes_{1_{j}\leq\mathtt{i}\leq k(j)_{j}}g^{d_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}}\otimes 1\big)=\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(g^{d_{i}}\otimes 1)

where
a_{j}=(\text{hei}(z\talloblong_{S_{1_{j}}}),\dots,\text{hei}(z\talloblong_{S_{k(j)_{j}}}))
and we have assumed each
h_{j}
factorizes as

h_{j}=\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\big(\bigtimes_{1_{j}\leq\mathtt{i}\leq k(j)_{j}}d_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\big),

i.e.
h_{j}
lies in the
F|_{T_{j}}
-face of the plate
[[K_{1_{j}};\dots;K_{k(j)_{j}}]]_{z_{j}}
. Notice that if the
h_{j}
do not factorize in this way, then
\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}(g^{h_{j}}\otimes 1)\otimes 1=0
. This map of modules is well-defined by
8.4
. It is an isomorphism because the map of
8.4
is injective.

We now make
{\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{l})}
explicit at the level of stalks. The stalk of
\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{l}})
at
x\in U_{K}
is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\mathcal{O}_{z_{j}}\big(\upmu_{F,G}(x)|_{T_{j}}\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}\big(\upmu_{F,G}(x)\big)\Big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(x)

=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\big\{g/f\in\mathcal{K}_{T_{j},\text{hei}(z_{j})}\ \big|\ g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{1_{j}};\cdots;K_{k(j)_{j}},z_{j}}],\,f(\upmu_{F,G}(x)|_{T_{j}})\neq 0\big\}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G}\big(\upmu_{F,G}(x)\big)\Big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(x).

The stalk of
\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}\talloblong_{S_{1}}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{l}\talloblong_{S_{k}}}
at
x\in U_{K}
is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big(\mathcal{O}_{z\talloblong_{S_{i}}}(x|_{S_{i}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(x)\big)

=\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\big(\big\{g/f\in\mathcal{K}_{S_{i},\text{hei}(z\talloblong_{S_{i}})}\ \big|\ g\in\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{K_{i},z\talloblong_{S_{i}}}],\,f(x|_{S_{i}})\neq 0\big\}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}(x)\big).

Then the map of stalks is given by extending the assignment

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}(g^{h_{j}}\otimes 1)\otimes 1=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\big(g^{\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}(\bigtimes_{1_{j}\leq\mathtt{i}\leq k(j)_{j}}d_{\mathtt{i}_{j}})}\otimes 1\big)\otimes 1\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\big(\bigotimes_{1_{j}\leq\mathtt{i}\leq k(j)_{j}}g^{d_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}}\otimes 1\big)=\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}(g^{d_{i}}\otimes 1)

\mathcal{O}_{F}(x)
-linearly. This gives a globally well-defined
{\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}
as we vary the affine chart
U_{K}
by comultiplication
\mathtt{f}
-naturality of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{O}}
. When checking the coherence laws for a homomorphism of bimonoids in

, we shall abbreviate this map as

(26)

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}h_{j}=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Big(\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}d_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\big)\Big)\,\mapsto\,\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}d_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}.

Theorem 8.6
.

The indexing
z\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{z}
of invertible sheaves on permutohedral space by Boolean functions, equipped with the
2
-cells
{\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}
and
{\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,G}}
as defined above, is a strong homomorphism of bimonoids

\varphi:\textsf{BF}\to\textsf{Mod}.

Proof.

There are nine coherence laws to check. Recall multiplication
\sigma
-naturality coherence says

\underbrace{\varphi_{G^{\prime}}({\mu_{F,G,\sigma}}_{z})}_{=\,\text{id}}\,\circ\,{\sigma}_{\mu_{F,G}(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}\circ{\mathtt{\Phi}^{\prime}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}={\mathtt{\Phi}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})^{\prime}}\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{F^{\prime},G^{\prime}}({\sigma}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})})\circ{\mu_{F,G,\sigma}}_{\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}}.

The left-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h_{i}=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\Big)\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{F^{\prime}|_{T^{\prime}_{j}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h^{\prime}_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\Big)

and the right-hand map is given by

{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}}h_{i}\mapsto{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}}h_{i}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h^{\prime}_{i}=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}h^{\prime}_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{F^{\prime}|_{T^{\prime}_{j}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h^{\prime}_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\Big).

Recall multiplication
\beta
-naturality coherence says

\underbrace{\varphi_{\widetilde{G}}({\mu_{F,G,\beta}}_{z})}_{=\,\text{id}}\,\circ\,{\beta_{G}}_{\mu_{F,G}(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}\circ{\widetilde{\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}={\mathtt{\Phi}_{\widetilde{G}F,\widetilde{G}}}_{\widetilde{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}}\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{\widetilde{G}F,\widetilde{G}}({\hat{\beta}}_{F}{\!\,}{}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})})\circ\mu_{\widetilde{G}F,\widetilde{G}}({\hat{\beta}}_{\mathtt{a}}){\circ\mu_{F,G,\beta}}_{\mathcal{O}_{z}}.

The left-hand map is given by
17
17

17

where the tilde
\widetilde{F}
refers to
\hat{\beta}

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h_{i}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\Big)\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{\widetilde{F}|_{T_{\beta(j)}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\hat{\beta}(\mathtt{i}_{j})}\Big)

and the right-hand map is given by

{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}}h_{i}\mapsto{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}}h_{i}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h_{\hat{\beta}(i)}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{\widetilde{F}|_{T_{\beta(j)}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\hat{\beta}(\mathtt{i}_{j})}\Big).

Comultiplication
\sigma
-naturality and
\beta
-naturality coherence follow similarly. Recall associativity coherence says

\underbrace{\varphi_{E}({\mu_{F,G,E}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})})}_{=\,\text{id}}\circ{\mathtt{\Phi}_{G,E}}_{\mu_{F,G}(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{G,E}({\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,G}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})})={\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,E}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}\circ{\mu_{F,G,E}}_{\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}}.

Let

F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{i},\dots,S_{k}),\qquad G=(T_{1},\dots,T_{j},\dots,T_{l}),\qquad E=(U_{1},\dots,U_{r},\dots,U_{e}).

Let
\mathtt{i}_{j}
be as before. Let
1\leq\mathtt{j}_{r}\leq l
be the integer such that

is the
\mathtt{j}

\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\text{m}_{G|_{U_{r}},a_{r}}\bigg(\bigtimes_{{\mathtt{j}}}\text{m}_{F|_{T_{\mathtt{j}_{r}}},a_{\mathtt{j}_{r}}}\Big(\bigtimes_{{\mathtt{i}}}h_{{\mathtt{i}_{\mathtt{j}}}_{r}}\Big)\bigg)

and the right-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h_{i}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h_{i}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\text{m}_{F|_{U_{r}},a_{r}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{r}}\Big).

However, we have

\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\text{m}_{G|_{U_{r}},a_{r}}\bigg(\bigtimes_{{\mathtt{j}}}\text{m}_{F|_{T_{\mathtt{j}_{r}}},a_{\mathtt{j}_{r}}}\Big(\bigtimes_{{\mathtt{i}}}h_{{\mathtt{i}_{\mathtt{j}}}_{r}}\Big)\bigg)=\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\text{m}_{F|_{U_{r}},a_{r}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{r}}\Big)

directly from the definition of

(the exact property we are using here is actually the associativity of the monoid mentioned in
8.1
). Coassociativity coherence says

\underbrace{\varphi_{F}({\Delta_{F,G,E}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{e})})}_{=\,\text{id}}\circ{\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,G}}_{\Delta_{G,E}(z_{1},\dots,z_{e})}\circ\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}({\mathtt{\Psi}_{G,E}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{e})})={\mathtt{\Psi}_{F,E}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{e})}\circ{\mu_{F,G,E}}_{\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{e})}}.

The left-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq{e}}h_{r}=\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq{e}}\text{m}_{G|_{U_{r}},a_{r}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{j}}d_{\mathtt{j}_{r}}\Big)\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{j}}d_{\mathtt{j}_{r}}=\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{j}}\text{m}_{F|_{T_{\mathtt{j}_{r}}},a_{\mathtt{j}_{r}}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}c_{{\mathtt{i}_{\mathtt{j}}}_{r}}\Big)

\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{j}}\bigotimes_{{\mathtt{i}}}c_{{\mathtt{i}_{\mathtt{j}}}_{r}}=\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}c_{\mathtt{i}_{r}}

and the right-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}h_{r}=\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\bigg(\text{m}_{F|_{U_{r}},a_{r}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}c_{\mathtt{i}_{r}}\Big)\bigg)\,\mapsto\,\bigotimes_{1\leq r\leq e}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}c_{\mathtt{i}_{r}}

(where we have again used the associativity of the monoid mentioned in
8.1
). Recall the bimonoid coherence law is

\varphi_{G}({\text{B}_{F,G,A}}_{\mathtt{a}})\circ{\mathtt{\Phi}_{GF,G}}_{\widetilde{\Delta_{FG,F}(z)}}\circ{\beta_{FG}}_{\Delta_{FG,F}(z)}\circ\Updelta^{\ast}_{GF,G}(\widetilde{{\mathtt{\Psi}_{FG,F}}_{z}})

={\mathtt{\Phi}_{GF,G}}_{\widetilde{\Delta_{FG,F}}(\mathtt{a})}\circ{\mathtt{\Psi}_{G,A}}_{\mu_{F,A}(z)}\circ\upmu^{\ast}_{G,A}({\mathtt{\Phi}_{F,A}}_{z})\circ{\text{B}_{F,G,A}}_{\varphi_{F}(\mathtt{a})}.

Let

F=(S_{1},\dots,S_{i},\dots,S_{k}),\quad G=(T_{1},\dots,T_{j},\dots,T_{l}),

FG=(X_{1},\dots,X_{n},\dots,X_{L}),\quad GF=(Y_{1},\dots,Y_{n},\dots,Y_{L}),\quad A=(A_{1},\dots,A_{m},\dots,A_{a}).

Let
1\leq\mathtt{i}_{m}\leq k
be the integer such that
S_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}
is the
\mathtt{i}
th lump of
F|_{A_{m}}
, let
1\leq\mathtt{j}_{m}\leq j
such that

is the
\mathtt{j}
th lump of
G|_{A_{m}}
, let
1\leq\mathtt{n}_{i}\leq L
such that
X_{\mathtt{n}_{i}}
is the
\mathtt{n}
th lump of
FG|_{S_{i}}
, and let
1\leq\mathtt{n}_{j}\leq L
such that
Y_{\mathtt{n}_{j}}
is the
\mathtt{n}
th lump of
GF|_{T_{j}}
. Let
1\leq\mathtt{n}_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}\leq L
such that
X_{\mathtt{n}_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}}
is the
\mathtt{n}
th lump of
FG|_{S_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}}
, and let

such that
Y_{\mathtt{n}_{\mathtt{j}_{m}}}
is the
\mathtt{n}
th lump of

.

Then the left-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h_{i}=\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\text{m}_{FG|_{S_{i}},a_{i}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{n}}d_{\mathtt{n}_{i}}\Big)\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{n}}d_{\mathtt{n}_{i}}

\mapsto\ \underbrace{\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{n}}d_{\beta(\mathtt{n}_{i})}=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{n}}e_{\mathtt{n}_{j}}}_{\text{where $e_{n}:=d_{\beta(n)}$}}\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{GF|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{n}}e_{\mathtt{n}_{j}}\Big)

and the right-hand map is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}h_{i}=\bigotimes_{1\leq m\leq a}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}

\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq m\leq a}\text{m}_{F|_{A_{m}},a_{m}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}\Big)=\bigotimes_{1\leq m\leq a}\text{m}_{F|_{A_{m}},a_{m}}\bigg(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}}\text{m}_{FG|_{S_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}},a_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{n}}d_{\mathtt{n}_{\mathtt{i}_{m}}}\Big)\bigg)

=\bigotimes_{1\leq m\leq a}\text{m}_{F|_{A_{m}},a_{m}}\bigg(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{j}}\text{m}_{GF|_{T_{\mathtt{j}_{m}}},a_{\mathtt{j}_{m}}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{n}}e_{\mathtt{n}_{\mathtt{j}_{m}}}\Big)\bigg)\ \mapsto\ \bigotimes_{1\leq m\leq a}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{j}}\text{m}_{GF|_{T_{\mathtt{j}_{m}}},a_{\mathtt{j}_{m}}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{n}}e_{\mathtt{n}_{\mathtt{j}_{m}}}\Big)

=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\text{m}_{GF|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\Big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{n}}e_{\mathtt{n}_{j}}\Big).\qed

Remark 8.4
.

The restriction of the homomorphism
\varphi:\textsf{BF}\to\textsf{Mod}
to the bimonoid generalized permutohedra
\textsf{GP}\hookrightarrow\textsf{BF}
, as defined in
[
AA17
, Section 5.1 & 12.3]
, is an indexing of the
nef invertible sheaves
, as a consequence of e.g.
[
Eur20
, Proposition 2.2.6]
.

8.7.
The Bialgebra of Global Sections of Invertible Sheaves

Let
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{BF}}
be the
BF
-graded bialgebra which is the pullback of the
Mod
-graded bialgebra
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{Mod}}
along the homomorphism
\varphi:\textsf{BF}\to\textsf{Mod}
, as constructed in
3.1
. Thus

\mathbb{C}\textbf{{BF}}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}[F]=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{k}}).

Let
\mathbb{C}\textbf{BF}
be the Joyalization of
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{BF}}
, thus

\mathbb{C}\textbf{BF}_{(z_{1},\dots,z_{k})}[F]:=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S_{1}}}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}})\otimes\dots\otimes\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S_{k}}}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{k}}).

We now show that
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{BF}}
is a strongly Joyal-theoretic bialgebra. The external product of global sections defines a morphism of
BF
-graded vector cospecies
\mathbb{C}\textbf{BF}\to\mathbb{C}\textbf{{BF}}
over the identity, given by

\text{prod}:\mathbb{C}\textbf{BF}\to\mathbb{C}\textbf{{BF}},\qquad s_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes s_{k}\,\mapsto\,s_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes s_{k}:=\pi_{S_{1}}^{\ast}s_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\pi_{S_{k}}^{\ast}s_{k}.

Proposition 8.7
.

The external product of global sections

\text{prod}:\mathbb{C}\textbf{BF}\to\mathbb{C}\textbf{{BF}},\qquad s_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes s_{k}\mapsto s_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes s_{k}

is an isomorphism of
BF
-graded vector cospecies.

Proof.

We have

\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S_{1}}}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}})\otimes\dots\otimes\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S_{k}}}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{k}})=\mathbb{C}\Big[\bigcap_{H_{1}\in\Sigma[S_{1}]}\text{M}_{H_{1},z_{1}}\Big]\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbb{C}\Big[\bigcap_{H_{k}\in\Sigma[S_{k}]}\text{M}_{H_{k},z_{k}}\Big].

Consider an affine open of the form
U_{H}=U_{H_{1}}\times\dots\times U_{H_{k}}
of
\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}
. Tensoring coherent sheaves corresponds to tensoring their modules on affine charts, and so

\Gamma_{U_{H}}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{k}})=(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{1},z_{1}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}])\otimes\dots\otimes(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{k},z_{k}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}).

We make the identification

(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{1},z_{1}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H}])\otimes\dots\otimes(\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{k},z_{k}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H})=\mathbb{C}[\text{M}_{H_{1},z_{1}}\times\dots\times\text{M}_{H_{k},z_{k}}].

Then

\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{F}}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{z_{k}})=\bigcap_{H}\mathbb{C}\Big[\text{M}_{H_{1},z_{1}}\times\dots\times\text{M}_{H_{k},z_{k}}\Big]=\mathbb{C}\Big[\bigcap_{H}\big(\text{M}_{H_{1},z_{1}}\times\dots\times\text{M}_{H_{k},z_{k}}\big)\Big].

Then the external product of global sections is given by the isomorphism of vector spaces

\mathbb{C}\Big[\bigcap_{H_{1}\in\Sigma[S_{1}]}\text{M}_{H_{1},z_{1}}\Big]\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbb{C}\Big[\bigcap_{H_{k}\in\Sigma[S_{k}]}\text{M}_{H_{k},z_{k}}\Big]\to\mathbb{C}\Big[\bigcap_{H}\big(\text{M}_{H_{1},z_{1}}\times\dots\times\text{M}_{H_{k},z_{k}}\big)\Big]

g^{h_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes g^{h_{k}}\mapsto g^{(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})}.\qed

Proposition 8.8
.

The
BF
-graded bialgebra
\mathbb{C}\textbf{{BF}}
is strongly Joyal-theoretic.

Proof.

For the multiplication, we need to show factorization

\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}\Big(\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}s_{i}\Big)=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Updelta^{\ast}_{F|_{T_{j}}}\Big(\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}s_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\Big).

We have that the multiplication pulled-back to
\mathbb{C}\textbf{\text{BF}}
along
prod
is given by

\Updelta^{\ast}_{F,G}:\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{S_{i}}}(\mathcal{O}_{z_{i}})\to\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}\Sigma^{T_{j}}}(\mathcal{O}_{(z_{1_{j}}|\dots|z_{k(j)_{j}})})

\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}s^{h_{i}}\mapsto\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}s^{\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\big)}

and indeed we have factorization because

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}s^{\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}\big(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}\big)}=\Updelta^{\ast}_{F|_{T_{j}}}\Big(\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}s^{h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}}\Big).

For the comultiplication, we need to show factorization

\upmu^{\ast}_{F,G}\Big(\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}s_{j}\Big)=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\upmu^{\ast}_{F|_{T_{j}}}(s_{j}).

We have that the comultiplication pulled-back to
\mathbb{C}\textbf{\text{BF}}
along
prod
is given by

\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}s^{h_{j}}=\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}s^{\text{m}_{F|_{T_{j}},a_{j}}(\bigtimes_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}})}\,\mapsto\,\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}s^{h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}}

and indeed we have factorization because

\bigotimes_{\mathtt{i}}s^{h_{\mathtt{i}_{j}}}=\upmu^{\ast}_{F|_{T_{j}}}(s^{h_{j}}).\qed
